On Self-Manifestation · Engineering Chapter · The Field Concretization Group Form · Chapter 19 v1.0

# The Discrimination Form of Trace-Response Transformation in Compartmentalized Third-Factor Circuits

1\. Purpose

### 1.1  Central Question

　In biological nervous systems: how the synaptic Trace-Responses that signals leave compose a set, how this set organizes activity, and how it goes on transforming in activity determined in part by history. In one sentence, throughout: what signals leave, how what is left organizes later activity, and how activity goes on transforming this set.  
　This paper takes compartmentalized third-factor circuits as its object class and the KC→MBON synapses of the *Drosophila* mushroom body (the short γ1pedc trace, the long α3 trace) as its main instance, and proceeds by the seven-step research procedure: find the Trace-Responses → the single Trace-Response → organization of the set → formation of tendency → tendency carried out as activity → activity acting again on the set → extracting the dynamics types and verification (Section 4).

### 1.2  What This Paper Establishes

　(1) The load-bearing clause of the Discrimination Form: Theorem 19-D (Section 6). On the frozen working domain 𝔇₁₉, the three candidates for the DAN reading within the consolidation window—the permissive type P, inhibitory return R₋ and excitatory return R₊—each have a closed-form forward map; the common window statistic Δ̄ (the within-window two-arm DAN difference between the trained arm and a stimulus-matched sham-trained arm) forces the three candidates to have pairwise disjoint outputs under antecedents D1–D4 and the resolution condition m \> 2e. What is to be discriminated is the kind of dynamics through which existing Trace-Responses are transformed; the Trace-Response set itself is given by the identification and organization criteria of *The Adjudication Form for Identifying Concretized Fields* v1.0.  
　(2) Conditional mechanism theorems for the seven steps: the three-way split of the single Trace-Response (Theorem 19-2), the old impeding the new (Proposition 19-3), the graded sign law (Theorem 19-4), inhibitory and excitatory self-loops (Theorems 19-5, 19-6), cross-compartment gating (Theorems 19-7, 19-7′, Corollary 19-7″), the issuing conditions for bypass rescue (Note 19-8), law-layer setting in the consolidation window (Theorem 19-10), internal continuation in the odor-free period (Theorem 19-11), the two bearings of extinction (Theorem 19-12), class-level conditional qualification and historical-dynamics diagnosis (Theorem 19-H).  
　(3) Theoretical claims T1–T10 (Section 9), each written in the form “this theory claims” with its chain of derivation; breachable predictions P19-1–P19-16 and preferred experiments ★1–★5 (Section 10).  
　(4) Qualification adjudication is an application of the criteria of *The Adjudication Form for Identifying Concretized Fields* v1.0, *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field* v1.0, *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* v1.0 and *The Adjudication Form of Material Field Concretization* v1.0; both the criteria and the dynamics types are taken from upstream.

### 1.3  Three Columns and Three Layers

　Three columns: A, the model column (model-mode verdicts on declared models, which may be 1, 0 or ⊥); B, the prediction column (conditional conclusions and breachable predictions for real hosts, with antecedents listed); C, the reality column (empirical certification; measurement mode gives only 1 or ⊥). The three columns stand apart: A is not read as B, nor B as C; ⊥ is not read as 0. A ⊥ in Column C means only that empirical certification has not yet been obtained, and does not downgrade the theoretical statement.  
　Three layers: the model layer (theorems and runs on declared models), the material evidence layer (published measurements, by citation level) and the duty layer (verdicts on the three duties and the master formula Fld of *The General Form of Field Concretization* v1.0). The three layers are written separately and are not inferred from one another; a missing experiment does not give a negative verdict. Theoretical predictions are not written as confirmed.  
　Marks: \[proved\] a proof is given in this paper; \[verified\] finite verification by the appendix programs (covering only the generated domain and not replacing proof; a number Qn refers to a verification item in the output of Appendix B); \[declared\] a declared item (frozen before adjudication, not proved by this paper). Evidence tags: \[Fact\] \[Published model\] \[Mechanism hypothesis\] \[Derivation\] \[Conditional conclusion\] \[Prediction\] \[Insufficient\] \[Representational choice\].

### 2.2  Upstream Programs Used

　Appendices A.1–A.6 are the frozen programs from the appendices of the upstream main texts (byte-for-byte identical to upstream): jhc\_core.py, jhc\_models.py, jhc\_bridge.py (Appendices A.1–A.3 of \[17\]); jxs\_core.py, jxs\_models.py, field\_final.py (Appendices A.1, A.2, A.4 of \[18\]; field\_final.py is byte-for-byte identical to Appendix A.1 of \[S2\]). Item Q0 of the appendix programs checks that their SHA-256 values match those listed upstream. Upstream programs are not modified; upstream conclusions are cited under the antecedents they list, and where an antecedent fails, nothing is issued.

### 2.3  Literature and Citation Levels

　Citation levels: “per the original Results and figure legends” means that the cited facts are taken from the Results section and figure legends of the original; “per the original main text and figure legends” is the same but includes the Discussion; “per the original abstract” goes only as far as the abstract states; “per the original accompanying model” refers to the model code and parameter files made public by the authors. Cited facts hold only within their level and are not promoted.  
　F1  Hige T, Aso Y, Modi MN, Rubin GM, Turner GC (2015) Heterosynaptic plasticity underlies aversive olfactory learning in Drosophila. Neuron 88:985–998. Per the original Results and figure legends: after a single pairing of odor with PPL1-γ1pedc activation, the firing of MBON-γ1pedc\>α/β (hereafter MVP2) to CS+ falls from 118 ± 8.3 to 24 ± 7.4 (n = 7, Fig. 1; the original reports spike counts per stimulus, mean ± SEM); under voltage clamp, CS+ charge transfer drops by 90% ± 3.7% (n = 5, Fig. 3), at the level of synaptic transmission; KC excitability and threshold are unchanged; reverse pairing is ineffective; the degree of depression varies with the KC overlap between the test odor and CS+ (r = 0.90, Fig. 7). Recordings lasted about 40 min.  
　F2–F5  Huang C, Luo J, Woo SJ, Roitman LA, Li J, Pieribone VA, Kannan M, Vasan G, Schnitzer MJ (2024) Dopamine-mediated interactions between short- and long-term memory dynamics. Nature 634:1141–1149. Per the original Results and figure legends: F2, the CS+ depression of MVP2 lasts less than 1 h and the CS+ depression of MBON-α3 more than 24 h, with intermediate tests before the 24 h reading; F3, the response of PPL1-α3 (and of PPL1-α′2α2) to CS+ turns from a decrease to an increase over three rounds of training and falls back 1 h later, and blocking MVP2 transmission during conditioning (Fig. 4i–j, n = 12 per group) abolishes this training dependence; the evoked response of PPL1-γ1pedc to CS+ falls relative to baseline (Fig. 3e, Extended Data Fig. 5a, attractive odor); F4, the extinction protocol (Fig. 5i–k): three rounds of unpaired CS+ and CS− presentations starting 10 min or 2 h after training, with the endpoint the MBON-α3 firing deviation 3 h after training (normalized to 5 min, n = 10 per group); Fig. 5j compares, at the 3 h endpoint, groups by the innate valence of the odor and by the two extinction onset times, and the reported extinction effects vary with valence and onset time; Fig. 5k shows separately an early enhancement for attractive odor at 35 min, kept apart from 5j; from 5j only the dependence on valence and onset time is taken, not its specific between-bar effects, and it is not read as a same-beat fact; F5, optogenetic activation of MVP2 during training impairs 3 h memory but not 5 min memory. The readings belong to different protocols or different groups of animals and do not form a measured chain animal by animal or beat by beat.  
　F6  Aso Y, Rubin GM (2016) Dopaminergic neurons write and update memories with cell-type-specific rules. eLife 5:e16135. Per the original Results and figure legends: optogenetic activation of PPL1-α3 paired with odor forms long-term memory; compartments differ in write rate, retention and update rules.  
　F7  Ichinose T, Aso Y, Yamagata N, Abe A, Rubin GM, Tanimoto H (2015) Reward signal in a recurrent circuit drives appetitive long-term memory formation. eLife 4:e10719. Per the original Results and figure legends: appetitive memory; blocking PAM-α1 output (Fig. 9A) or MBON-α1 output (Fig. 9C) for 1 h immediately after training impairs 24 h memory, while a blockade of the same length 22 h later does not (Fig. 9B, 9D); blocking PAM-α1 during training impairs long-term memory (Fig. 3D). This is a within-window manipulation of synaptic output, not a record of within-window activity differences.  
　F8  Pavlowsky A, Schor J, Plaçais P-Y, Preat T (2018) A GABAergic feedback shapes dopaminergic input on the Drosophila mushroom body to promote appetitive long-term memory. Current Biology 28 (doi 10.1016/j.cub.2018.04.040). Per the original main text and figure legends: appetitive memory; MVP2 output within about 0.5–1 h after training is required for long-term memory, and it terminates the oscillatory activity of MP1 (PPL1-γ1pedc); MVP2 → MP1 is a GABAergic return.  
　F9  Yan L, Wu L, Wiggin TD, Su X, Yan W, Li H, Li L, Lu Z, Guo F, Meng Z, Li Y, Li F, Griffith LC, Liu C (2026) Brief disruption of activity in a subset of dopaminergic neurons during consolidation impairs long-term memory by fragmenting sleep. eLife 14:RP104862. Per the original Results and figure legends: an appetitively trained group and an untrained (vehicle) group, recorded continuously for 3 h after training with TRIC-LUC and summarized hourly (Fig. 8C–F; PAM-α1 driver MB299B; n = 20–24 per round, three rounds); within 3 h after training, PAM-α1 activity does not differ significantly from baseline.  
　F10  Felsenberg J et al. (2018) Integration of parallel opposing memories underlies memory extinction. Cell 175:709–722.e15. Per the original abstract: extinction of an aversive memory is implemented by a parallel, rewarding extinction memory, and the two are integrated in the MBON network.  
　F11  The model accompanying Huang et al. 2024 (per the original accompanying model: code and parameter files): MVP2 → PPL1-α3 has a negative weight and MBON-α3 → PPL1-α3 a positive weight, with a signed linear learning rule and the time constant of the α module switching 3 h after the last training. The only data relied on are data\_and\_parameters/Imaging\_24hr\_data.xlsx (group means and SEM of the 24 h imaging) in the original's accompanying model repository (schnitzer-lab/Luo\_Huang\_2024\_MB\_model), together with the parameter files; the original has further public channels: the figure-by-figure Source Data listed under Data availability, and the Zenodo record 10.5281/zenodo.10998457 (neural voltage-activity traces and behavioral data for the five main figures). These records have not yet been compiled animal by animal under the declared criterion and recomputed (U19-6).

3\. Object and Declared Models  
3.1  Object of Study and Declared Instances \[declared\]  
　Main instance χ\_γα3: the mushroom body of one hemisphere of adult *Drosophila*; B contains the KC axon segments of the two compartments γ1pedc and α3, MVP2, PPL1-γ1pedc (MP1), MBON-α3, PPL1-α3 and their mutual synapses; T = T₁ ∪ T₂, where T₁ is the depression of synaptic efficacy from the CS+-activated KC subset onto MVP2 and T₂ the depression from the same subset onto MBON-α3; W runs from training to beyond 24 h; P is the preparation of F2–F5. The formation event is three rounds of CS+ paired with shock; the observation set is that paper's voltage imaging (MVP2, MBON-α3, PPL1-γ1pedc, PPL1-α3), transmission blockade, optogenetic activation during training, and the unpaired presentations 10 min and 2 h after training.  
　Auxiliary instances: χ\_γ (γ1pedc single compartment, the optogenetic pairing of F1); χ\_α (α1, F7 and Yamada et al. 2023); χ\_ext (extinction γ1pedc → γ5, F10; a separate declaration, not spliced with χ\_γα3).  
　Comparisons: *C. elegans* ASER→AIB and AFD→AIY; human hippocampal and neocortical STDP; cerebellar PF→PC. These serve only as structural comparisons and do not enter the certificates of the *Drosophila* instances.

### 3.2  Declared Models \[declared\]

　(1) The Fly models of the appendix of \[17\] (full, gonly, gfb) and the FLY, FLYC, LOOP and CHAIN models of the appendix of \[18\] (Appendices A.1–A.6, i.e. the frozen upstream programs).  
　(2) The binary class 𝒦\_B (Definition 19-0) and its six frozen members Φ\_γ, Φ\_γα3^0, Φ\_γα3^±, Φ\_α^I, Φ\_α^II, Φ\_sr (Appendices A.7, A.8).  
　(3) The graded class 𝒩₃F (Definition 19-1), the consolidation-window model Consol (Definition 19-1′), the frozen candidates WindowCand of Theorem 19-D (Section 5) and the cerebellar comparison member Cereb (Appendix A.9).  
　(4) All parameters are constructed values (dimensionless rationals), not measured values; predictions about real objects use only qualitative structure (signs, order, equality relations) and explicitly stated margin conditions.  
Definition 19-0 (the binary class 𝒦\_B) \[Representational choice\]  
　On top of Definitions 5 and 7 of \[S13\] (𝒦\_PA^3, 𝒦\_PA^≺): (1) the relaxation coefficients ρ\_ij are declared per Structural Position; (2) the gating units for potentiation and depression are declared separately per column, gp\[j\] (used by PLUS) and gm\[j\] (used by MINUS); everything else (entries, gating units, the same-beat directed acyclic firing input U, the beat-wise order, clipping) is unchanged. Beat-wise order: read in s; unit by unit in topological order, vt\_j = λ\_j v\_j + Σ\_i w\_ij s\_i + Σ\_{k≺j} U\_kj g\_k + I\_j, g\_j = \[vt\_j ≥ θ\_j\]; reset; x′ = d\_x x + s, y′ = d\_y y + g; P⁺\_ij = A₊ x′\_i (when g\_{gp\[j\]}), P⁻\_ij = −A₋ y′\_{gm\[j\]} (when s\_i), P^r\_ij = −ρ\_ij(w\_ij − b\_ij); clipping to \[lo, hi\]. Coarse-beat reading: λ = 0, v\_reset = 0, d\_x = d\_y = 0; one beat is one odor-presentation slot, and the binary firing of an MBON or DAN is read as “the firing rate within that slot crosses the declared threshold”. Frozen members: 4 KC entries (CS+ activates A = {0, 1}, CS− activates {2, 3}); the KC→MBON positions take the depression-depth reading, with baseline b = hi = 1/2 and depth s = b − w ∈ \[0, 1/2\]; the KC→DAN positions are static drives.  
　Subclass 𝒦\_C (single compartment): gating units M (MBON), D (DAN) and a never-firing Z, topological order M ≺ D, U\_MD = u; potentiation and depression in column M are both gated by D; write c\_A := Σ\_{i∈A} c\_i (the sum of the static KC→D drives), s\* := (n\_A·hi − θ\_M)/n\_A, dm := A₋ − A₊. Subclass 𝒦\_X (cross-compartment): gating units M1 (MVP2), D1 (PPL1-γ1pedc), M2 (MBON-α3), D2 (PPL1-α3), \[D2m\], Z; U₁₁ := U\_{M1,D1} \< 0, U₁₂ := U\_{M1,D2} \< 0, \[U\_m := U\_{M1,D2m} \> 0\]; gm(M1) = D1, gm(M2) = D2, and gp(M2) = Z (binary members) or D2m (signed members).  
Definition 19-1 (the graded class 𝒩₃F of compartmentalized third-factor circuits) \[Representational choice\]  
　Structural Positions x = (c, i): compartment c ∈ C, KC i ∈ {1, …, N}; depth d\_x ∈ \[0, 1\] (depression type: efficacy w = w⁰(1 − d); potentiation type: w = w⁰(1 + d)). A signal s ∈ 𝒮 gives each KC an activation a\_i(s) ∈ \[0, 1\] (odors, the background signal bg, rest); a reinforcement signal s⁺ additionally injects US\_c into the DAN of the declared compartment.  
　Read-out (activity): MBON\_c = R\_c(Σ\_i a\_i(s)·w\_{c,i}/w⁰), with R\_c monotonically nondecreasing (declared members take R\_c(z) = max(0, z − θ\_c)); DAN\_c = Q\_c(β\_c + US\_c·\[s⁺\] + Σ\_{c′} U\_{c′c}·MBON\_{c′}), with Q\_c monotonically nondecreasing (taken as max(0, ·)).  
　Write: W\_x = η\_c·a\_i(s)·G\_c(DAN\_c), with G\_c monotonically nondecreasing (unsigned: G(z) = z; signed: G(z) = z − θ\*\_c, potentiating when DAN is below the zero point, i.e. the depression-type Trace-Response shallows); relaxation −ρ\_c·d\_x (a direct read of its own position); the post-processing is position-wise clipping to \[0, 1\] (LP of \[18\]).  
　Status: every member of 𝒩₃F is a declared host model in the sense of D18-1 of \[18\], satisfying LW (the write reads only the depth at its own position, the DAN of its own compartment and the activation of its own KC) and LP. FLY of \[18\] (PPL1-α3 = −a + bΣd\_γ1, α3 write = η·PPL1-α3) is a signed, untruncated member form of it; 𝒦\_C is the limiting form in which R and Q are step functions (not in 𝒩₃F; carried by 𝒦\_B). The model accompanying F11 is structurally of the same type as the signed members of 𝒩₃F (steady-state firing rates within segments, truncated-linear MBONs, a signed linear learning rule); its time-constant switch is a clock-controlled law layer (4.6.5); the run results of that model are not included in the evidence of this paper (U19-6).  
Definition 19-1′ (the consolidation-window model Consol) \[Representational choice\]  
　Compartment c of 𝒩₃F with a law-layer component c\_x ∈ {0, 1} (consolidation state) added: relaxation d′ = ρ(c\_x)·d (retention ratios ρ(1) \> ρ(0); write ρ₀ := ρ(0), ρ₁ := ρ(1)); under the post-training window signal bg, each KC has activation b ≥ 0; within the window, MBON = R(Σ\_i b(1 − d\_i) − θ\_M) and DAN\_w = Q(κ + u·MBON); the consolidation write CONS\_x has three gates—the DAN gate DAN\_w ≥ θ\_c, the tag gate d\_x \> τ\_tag (reading the depth at its own position) and the settable gate c\_x = 0—and when all three hold at once, c\_x ← 1. u = 0 is the permissive type, u \< 0 inhibitory return, u \> 0 excitatory return.  
3.3  Cross-Check of the Binary Class against the Running System of \[18\] \[verified Q2e\]  
　KCAdapter (Appendix A.9) independently rewrites the coarse-beat members of 𝒦\_B as hosts in the running system of \[18\]: the complete state contains only the depth d = b − w, the read-out computes the gates in topological order, the write components are given process by process per Equation 4.2 of \[18\] (PLUS, MINUS, RELAX), and the post-processing is clipping (LP). Over 120 random members × 25 beats (3000 beats), the post-states and gates of jxs\_core.step of \[18\] agree beat by beat with those of k17\_core.step (Q2e). Hence conclusions on 𝒦\_B can be stated in the notation of \[18\].  
4\. Unfolding the Seven-Step Research Procedure

### 4.1  Step 1  Find the Trace-Responses

4.1.1  Signal, Medium and the Change Left  
　Signal: odors activate KC subsets via projection neurons (sparse coding; the KC subsets of different odors overlap); reinforcement (shock, sugar) acts through compartment-specific dopaminergic neurons (PPL1 cluster: γ1pedc, α3, etc.; PAM cluster: α1, γ5, etc.) (F1, F6, F2–F5). The unconditioned stimulus acts from outside B, meeting the out-of-boundary condition on signal sources of \[S1\]; unpaired CS+ after training carries only the odor signal, and its write is borne by in-field DANs.  
　Medium: the synaptic arrays formed in each compartment by KC axons onto the dendrites of that compartment's MBONs; one KC axon passes through several compartments, and each compartment has its own write rules (F1, F6).  
　Change left: after pairing, MVP2 firing to CS+ falls from 118 ± 8.3 to 24 ± 7.4, CS+ charge transfer under voltage clamp drops by 90% ± 3.7%, and KC excitability and threshold are unchanged—the change is in synaptic transmission, not in the KCs (\[Fact\] F1); reverse pairing is ineffective (\[Fact\] F1). The CS+ firing depression of MBON-α3 lasts more than 24 h (\[Fact, firing layer\] F2). Nonzero firing does not by itself certify that the read-out lies in its linear regime; a recording of about 40 min is not the clamped self-retention lower bound of Definition 2′ of \[17\].  
　Retained change is kept apart from concurrent activity: firing is activity occurring at the time; synaptic efficacy is the retained change. Lemma 17-1′ of \[17\] on the declared model (Q1d): with the Trace-Responses clamped and the odor withdrawn, MBON-α3 activity is identically 0 (activity has no self-retention); unclamped, the participation effect under the CS+ probe does not vanish within 300 beats. What is retained is the synaptic Trace-Response; “the MBON response remains reduced after training” is a read-out of the Trace-Response, not retention of the activity itself.  
4.1.2  Trace-Response–Carrier–Transformation Correspondence Table  
　(Columns: Trace-Response \| bearing carrier \| what bears formation (deepening) \| retention (scale and evidence layer) \| strengthening \| weakening (shallowing) \| saturation and limit \| evidence layer)  
　T₁ γ1pedc depression \| KC→MVP2 synaptic efficacy on (KC\_i, γ1pedc) \| coincidence of CS+ KC firing × PPL1-γ1pedc dopamine (third factor; reverse pairing ineffective) \[Fact\] F1 \| at least about 40 min (F1), less than 1 h (F2), the time courses of the two papers set side by side \[Fact\] \| repeated pairing deepens it to 80%–90% \[Fact\] \| natural recovery \[Fact\]; unpaired DAN activation potentiates MBON responses (γ4, Cohn et al. 2015) \[Fact, other compartment\] \| saturation near bottom; on recovery to baseline the Trace-Response phenomenon does not occur \| direct measurement of synaptic currents.  
　T₂ α3 depression \| KC→MBON-α3 synaptic efficacy on (KC\_i, α3) \[Mechanism hypothesis: inferred from the firing layer\] \| during training, CS+ × PPL1-α3 (in F11 shock drives PPL1-α3 directly \[Published model\]); early after training, unpaired CS+ disinhibits PPL1-α3 via the γ1 Trace-Response (F3) \| \> 24 h (firing layer) \[Fact\]; the 3 h time-constant switch of F11 \[Published model\] \| under the extinction protocol the α3 plasticity readings vary with odor valence and extinction onset time (F4, Fig. 5j) \[Fact\] \| as at left; whether strengthening and weakening are borne by the same synapse or by parallel layers is undetermined \| not measured \| firing layer; synaptic layer not measured directly \[Insufficient\].  
　T\_α1 α1 depression \| KC→MBON-α1 synaptic efficacy on (KC\_i, α1) \| CS+ × PAM-α1 pairing (Yamada et al. 2023) \| 24 h memory depends on MBON-α1 and PAM-α1 output during training and 0–1 h after training (F7) \[Fact, behavioral layer\]; 24 h retention at the synaptic layer not measured directly \[Insufficient\] \| — \| — \| — \| firing after pairing, and behavior.  
　T\_ext extinction Trace-Response \| a rewarding opposing memory parallel to the original Trace-Response (F10, per the original abstract) \| extinction protocol \| — \| — \| — \| — \| parallel opposing Trace-Response.  
　T\_γ5⁺ γ5 potentiation \| dopamine-dependent potentiation of KC→MBON-γ5β′2a (Zhao et al. 2018) \| potentiation-type orientation \| — \| strengthened by repeated activation, plateau \| — \| — \| comparison material.  
　c consolidation state (law-layer component) \| the bearer of the change in write-law coefficients (shallowing rate) with post-training activity or time; molecule and location undetermined \[Mechanism hypothesis\] \| circuit output 0–1 h after training is required (F7, F8) \[Fact, behavioral layer\]; F11 represents it by a clock-controlled time-constant switch \[Published model\] \| — \| — \| — \| — \| D18-3 of \[18\]: if written by the signal history and retained, it is a Trace-Response by definition.  
　Not Trace-Responses (carriers or activity, not entering T): KC, MBON and DAN firing (activity); KC odor adaptation (activity residue); the existence of synapses and connections, the KC→MBON connection graph, the MBON→DAN and MBON→MBON wiring (background b; the wiring of organization, Scheffer et al. 2020, Li et al. 2020).  
　Carrier and Trace-Response distinguished: “all the synapses exist” is no proof of a change left by signals. Only a change in efficacy relative to the trace-free ground state (the same preparation, untrained or sham-trained) that is caused by the signal history through the write law and retained within W is a Trace-Response (D18-3 of \[18\]; Definitions 1 and 4′ of \[17\]). Q1c: after one pairing, the positive candidates are exactly the signal-formation positions (the γ1 and α3 positions of CS+); the KC positions of CS− do not enter.  
　Comparisons (transfer conditions; not entering the *Drosophila* certificates): the experience-dependent change in transmission at *C. elegans* ASER→AIB with salt concentration, and the temperature memory at AFD→AIY (Kunitomo et al. 2013, Hawk et al. 2018)—the Trace-Response–carrier correspondence can be written out in the same way, but no compartmentalized third-factor write gating has been seen, so they are not declared as members of 𝒩₃F; STDP in human hippocampus ex vivo and in human neocortex (Testa-Silva et al. 2010, Verhoog et al. 2013) is of potentiation type and self-gated, corresponding to the continued-writing cell of the sign law; for cerebellar PF→PC LTD see 4.7.4. Theorems 19-4 and 19-H are applied only when the object has, in addition, independent evidence for all three of “a read-out-position Trace-Response, a teaching modulatory signal, a return from read-out to teaching”; otherwise it serves only as a structural analogy.  
4.1.3  Three-Way Split and Set Boundary \[verified Q1a, Q1b, Q1e\]  
　Theorem 17-1 of \[17\] on the declared model: the γ1 positions have complete self-retention of 29 beats (tail evidence: a forward-invariant subthreshold set), while the α3 positions obtain only a lower bound ≥ 300 beats. With τ\_act = 1: for τ\_W = 12 and 29, γ1 is a Trace-Response carrier, and for τ\_W = 60 it is activity residue; α3 is positively determined as a Trace-Response carrier under all three, by its lower bound (Q1b). Hence “the short γ1 trace is a Trace-Response” is a verdict relative to the declared window length: T = γ1pedc ∪ α3 is meaningful only because unpaired CS+ 10 min after training falls within the γ1 retention period; within the window of the 24 h reading, γ1 is already activity residue. On the binary member Φ\_γα3^0, T₁ obtains complete self-retention of 25 beats with a geometric envelope as tail evidence, while T₂ (ρ₂ = 0) obtains only a lower bound (Q1e).  
　Set boundary: the declared γ1pedc ∪ α3 is a sub-organization with boundary conditions within the full connectome (Theorem 17-4′(4) of \[17\]); PPL1-α′2α2 also reads MBON-γ1pedc output (the connectome and the negative weight in F11), and whether it returns to T is an antecedent of the in-boundary self-feedback type verdict (U19-10); it does not affect the adjudication of basic qualification.  
Proposition 19-1 (identification of synaptic Trace-Responses) \[proved (direct application); verified Q1\]  
　At declared window length τ\_W and resolution ε, if (i) the depression of KC→MBON efficacy relative to the trace-free ground state is formed by the pairing signal history (at the formation positions the comparison with the reference history exceeds ε), (ii) with the other carriers clamped, the self-retention lower bound is ≥ τ\_W, and (iii) K1–K3 hold for the declared probe CS+ and the read-out MBON drive, then this depression is a Trace-Response as recognized by the element certificate of Definition 4′ of \[17\]; MBON and DAN firing at the same moment is not a Trace-Response (Lemma 17-1′ of \[17\]).  
　Proof: a direct application of Definitions 2′, 3′, 4′ and Lemma 17-1′ of \[17\]. ∎  
　On the real object: in γ1pedc, (i) and (iii) are borne by synaptic-current measurements, while (ii) reaches only the observational lower bound “at least about 40 min”; for α3, (i)–(iii) all reach only the firing layer.  
4.2  Step 2  The Single Trace-Response: Deepening, Shallowing, Zero Change, Saturation, Crossing the Baseline and Addition  
4.2.1  Depth and Write Conditions \[verified Q2a, Q2b\]  
　Depth reading \[declared\]: depression type, d = 1 − w/w⁰ (the reading s = b − w of 𝒦\_B differs from it by a constant factor when b = w⁰). K2 of \[17\] holds relative to the probe CS+ and the read-out MBON drive (order conflicts for γ1 and α3: 0); for the probe CS− (which does not activate these KCs) every nonzero depth is hidden—depth is issued relative to the declared probe class (Q2a). K3 holds only for the declared write conditions: γ1 is not erased by repetition under “CS+ paired with reinforcement” and shallows under unpaired CS+; the K3 of α3 under unpaired CS+ depends on the common depth of γ1 (holding in the declared model when it is ≥ 1/4)—“deepening under repeated exposure” is not a property of α3 by itself, but of α3 together with its organization (the short γ1 trace and the MBON-γ1pedc → PPL1-α3 return) (Q2b). This is one concrete form, on the neural side, of the ontological explanatory note “The Trace-Response set is organized by dynamics, and its transformation rests on the deepening and shallowing of its elements”.  
4.2.2  Laws of Change of the Single Trace-Response  
Lemma 19-2a (common depth and one-beat law on 𝒦\_C) \[proved; verified Q2e, Q8a, Q8b, Q11q\]  
　Setting: 𝒦\_C; λ = 0, v\_reset = 0, d\_x = d\_y = 0; the KC→M positions have baseline b = hi and depth s = hi − w ∈ \[0, hi\]; the KC→D positions are static drives c\_i; the relaxation ratio of column M is ρ ∈ \[0, 1); depression component A₋ ≥ 0, potentiation component A₊ ≥ 0. CS+ activates the KC set A (\|A\| = n\_A), and the activation sets of the other letters are disjoint from A (C0); injections go only into D and are nonnegative; Z is frozen as never firing (θ\_Z above all its reachable inputs), and there is no injection into Z. Within-column uniformity (C1): the positions of A have the same relaxation ratio, A₋, A₊, clipping \[0, hi\] and plasticity gate.  
　Statement: under C0, C1 and the zero initial state, the depths at the positions of A are always equal; call the common value s. (1) On a CS+ beat, M fires ⟺ s ≤ s\* (assuming 0 ≤ s\* \< hi). (2) On a CS+ beat, the gating input of D is vt\_D = c\_A + u·\[s ≤ s\*\] + I\_D. (3) On a CS+ beat, if D fires then s′ = clip((1 − ρ)s + dm, 0, hi), and if it does not, s′ = (1 − ρ)s; on non-CS+ beats s′ = (1 − ρ)s. When dm ≥ 0 the lower clip has no effect and s′ = min(hi, (1 − ρ)s + dm); when dm \< 0 the lower clip can be hit (example Q11q: s = 0, A₊ = 1/4 \> A₋ = 1/8; the unclipped expression gives −1/8, while the actual post-state is 0).  
　Proof: the positions of A are activated together, and only on letters containing A; C1 makes the components, relaxation and clipping they receive the same; Z does not fire, so the columns gated by Z are static, and induction from equal initial values gives identity. (1) The input of M is Σ\_A w = n\_A(hi − s) ≥ θ\_M ⟺ s ≤ s\*. (2) D reads Σ\_A c\_i = c\_A, plus u·g\_M and the injection. (3) When D fires, the sum of the components at its own position is A₊ − A₋ − ρ(w − hi), i.e. hi − w′ = (1 − ρ)s + dm; weight clipping w′ ∈ \[0, hi\] gives s′ ∈ \[0, hi\]; when D does not fire, the MINUS component is −A₋·0 and PLUS does not occur, leaving only relaxation. A non-CS+ beat does not activate A: MINUS does not occur and the PLUS component is A₊·0, leaving only relaxation. ∎  
Theorem 19-2 (three-way split of the single Trace-Response: write action, comparison with the control, actual net change) \[proved; verified Q2f, Q2g, Q10o\]  
　Assume the original-mechanism semantics of \[18\] (D18-8(A)), with pre-beat depth d at position x, component c\_w of the write process ρ\_w, sum c\_o of the other components, and post-processing the clipping clip to \[lo, hi\]; write z := d + c\_o.  
　(1) Write action: x ∈ Act^ε(ρ\_w) ⟺ \|c\_w\| \> ε; neither this nor x ∈ Aff⁰ (d′ ≠ d, covering both deepening and shallowing) implies the other.  
　(2) Relative to the no-write control: strictly deeper ⟺ c\_w \> 0 ∧ z \< hi ∧ z + c\_w \> lo; strictly shallower ⟺ c\_w \< 0 ∧ z \> lo ∧ z + c\_w \< hi; in all other cases the value equals that of the control. Example X7 (Q10o): z = −1/2, c\_w = 1/4; both arms are clipped to 0.  
　(3) Actual net change: d′ \> d ⟺ clip(z + c\_w) \> d; d′ \< d ⟺ clip(z + c\_w) \< d; x ∈ Aff⁰ ⟺ clip(z + c\_w) ≠ d.  
　(4) On 𝒦\_X (lo = 0, hi the baseline, c\_o = −ρ₂s₂, so z ∈ \[0, hi\]), with the write gate true as antecedent (PPL1-α3 fires, i.e. s₁ \> s₁\*, Theorem 19-7(1)): c\_w = A₋ \> 0; deeper than the control ⟺ (1 − ρ₂)s₂ \< hi; actual net deepening ⟺ s₂ \< hi ∧ A₋ \> ρ₂s₂. When the write gate is false, c\_w = 0 and none of the three holds.  
　Proof: (1) By definition. (2) clip is monotonically nondecreasing; for c\_w \> 0, clip(z + c\_w) \> clip(z) ⟺ the interval (z, z + c\_w\] intersects the strictly increasing segment (lo, hi\] of clip ⟺ z \< hi and z + c\_w \> lo; c\_w \< 0 is symmetric. (3) By direct comparison. (4) Substitute z = (1 − ρ₂)s₂ ≥ 0; z + A₋ \> lo always holds. ∎  
　Example X1 (Q2g): ρ₂ = 1; the MINUS component 1/8 and the RELAX component −1/8 cancel, so the α3 position is in Act^ε but not in Aff⁰. Over 400 random members of type Φ\_γα3: written 301, deeper than the control 282, net deepening 271, written without net deepening 30, disagreements with the criteria 0 (Q2f).  
　Reading: “a write component occurs” is action (Act), and “actual net deepening” is being affected (Aff); the gap between the two is what \[18\] calls “cancellation is maintenance under the action of dynamics”.  
4.2.3  Boundary Cases \[verified Q2c, Q2d, Q2h, Q2i\]  
　Saturation: pairing again at the upper end of clipping gives a nonzero component and an unchanged post-state (the zero change is borne by clipping, Q2c). Shallowing until the Trace-Response phenomenon does not occur: after 29 rest beats the γ1 positions no longer exceed ε, and thereafter stay so by a forward-invariant subthreshold set (Q2d). Crossing the baseline: if a signed write is not clipped at baseline, a depression-type Trace-Response crosses the baseline and becomes of potentiation type; it is listed as a reverse candidate under Definition 4′ of \[17\], on its own, and is not counted in the original orientation (Q2h); 𝒦\_B truncates it at baseline by w ≤ hi = b, which is a \[Representational choice\], not a real impossibility. Addition: the generation of Structural Positions is represented within the run of \[18\] by existence variables (D18-12, Theorem 18-9(3); Q2i shows the form with the CHAIN model); whether long-term memory in *Drosophila* is accompanied by the formation of new KC→MBON synapses has no direct evidence in the cited material \[Insufficient\] and is not declared (U19-8). Multiple state variables: for one and the same KC, its γ1 and α3 KC × compartment positions are each a separate state quantity; the law-layer component c is another state quantity (4.6.5); the separate bearing by presynaptic and postsynaptic sides has not been resolved (F1 excludes only KC excitability) \[Insufficient\].  
　Shallowing mechanisms listed separately: (A) natural recovery (the relaxation component reading its own position directly; the main shallowing of γ1); (B) unpaired DAN, or DAN below the zero point under a signed write (activity-mediated; a candidate for the late weakening of α3); (C) cancellation by a parallel opposing layer (cancellation at the read-out layer, not shallowing of the original Trace-Response, Theorem 19-12). The three can have the same shape in the readings (a late rebound of the MBON-α3 response); for their discrimination see P19-13.  
4.3  Step 3  Organization of the Set: Overlap, Compartments, Convergence and Intervention between Subsets  
4.3.1  What Bears Organization \[verified Q3a–Q3c\]  
　Organization description (D18-4 of \[18\]) = placement of the Trace-Responses on labeled KC × compartment positions + the recruited subset K(s) of each odor and their overlap + convergence of the synapses of one compartment onto one MBON + inter-compartment MBON→DAN return and inhibition between MBONs + the per-compartment rules of write, shallowing and (if any) consolidation. The connection graph itself is background and does not by itself constitute organization (Theorem 18-2(3) of \[18\]: isomorphism of connection graphs does not suffice for identity of organization).  
　\[Fact\] The KC subsets of different odors overlap, and the degree of depression varies with the KC overlap with CS+ (F1, r = 0.90); MBON-γ1pedc is an inhibitory input to PPL1-α3, and blocking its transmission during conditioning abolishes the training-dependent response of PPL1-α3 (F3).  
　Theorem 17-4′ of \[17\] on the declared model: with only A trained, the CS+ positions of γ1 and α3 form one component (through the history-condition hyperedge via PPL1-α3, Q3a); with disjoint KCs there are two components, with overlapping KCs one (Q3b). “Overlap determines the organization boundary” is issued only for the verified model and the declared protocol (the CS+ and CS− probes and the declared joint-setting family); overlap is a possible pathway, and being off the plateau and crossing the threshold are the antecedents of its taking effect (Proposition 19-3(1)). Theorem 18-9(1) of \[18\]: effective overlap is derived from depth; when the depression at shared KC positions reaches its limit, effective overlap vanishes and the tendency at the overlap probe changes (Q3c).  
　Intervention between subsets: in the verified FLY model, I(α3 ← γ1) ≠ 0 and I(γ1 ← α3) = 0 (Q4c), the direction being determined by the declared return wiring; F11 additionally has a positive weight MBON-α3 → PPL1-α3, and under that declaration α3 has a within-compartment return (P19-7).  
Proposition 19-3 (the old impedes the new: an old Trace-Response on the overlap weakens a new write through return) \[proved; verified Q3d, Q3e, Q10p, Q11r\]  
　Take a single-compartment member of 𝒩₃F (depression type, excitatory return U\_cc = u \> 0, unsigned write) in which the old odor A has already left depth on K(A), and the new odor B overlaps A on K(A) ∩ K(B).  
　(1) Organization (the conditions for a possible pathway to take effect): if (A) the MBON input of B lies in the linear regime of R both in the actual state and in the control state (overlap positions set back to baseline), (B) the DAN input lies in the linear regime of Q, and (C) the total component difference exceeds ε, and the control belongs to the declared family of legitimate joint settings, then the write process at a position x exclusive to B has a history-condition hyperedge with the overlap positions and x as vertices (Definition 5′ of \[17\]). Issuing a hyperedge requires taking a minimal subset of settings that makes the difference cross the threshold: if the declared control contains redundant positions, the threshold crossing of the whole joint setting shows only that at least one minimal witness exists, and does not issue all positions of the whole setting as vertices (example Q11r: overlap positions of depth 1/2 and 1/50; setting the former back gives a difference of 1/8, setting the latter back 1/200, and the minimal subset contains only the former). When any condition fails there may be no hyperedge (example X8, Q10p: the MBON threshold 3 is not less than the number of activated KCs, R stays on its lower plateau, and setting an overlap position back from 1/2 to 0 gives a total difference of 0). When the B positions have not yet formed before the write, this hyperedge is listed only as a potential influence; to issue a real organization component, one must prove, within the declared set window, that the B positions form and meet element qualification (Definition 4′). Q3d is constructed with the listed candidate vertices, and what it proves is a candidate graph.  
　(2) Direction: under (A) and (B), the write component of the first B pairing at x is monotonically nonincreasing in the depth of the overlap positions (Theorem 19-4); it is strictly decreasing when the composite difference over the actual input interval is nonzero (Q3e: 7/16 → 3/16).  
　(3) Binary limit (Type I of 𝒦\_C): the first B pairing writes if and only if o·s\_A ≤ n·s\*; blockade of MBON→DAN during B training prevents B's Trace-Response from forming (the blockade does not lift the impediment; it makes the second write not occur at all).  
　Proof: (1) The write at x is η·a\_x·G(Q(β + US + u·R(Σ\_{K(B)} (1 − d\_i)))) (US counted on training beats), and the depths of the overlap positions enter Σ\_{K(B)}; in the non-plateau regions of R, Q and G, changing the depth of the overlap positions changes the total component at x, which gives a history-condition hyperedge by Definition 5′ of \[17\]; without overlap, the component at x reads no position of A. (2) Theorem 19-4. (3) Under Type I (teaching alone insufficient: J + c\_B \< θ\_D ≤ J + c\_B + u), on a B pairing beat Σ\_{K(B)} w = n·hi − o·s\_A, and M fires ⟺ o·s\_A ≤ n·s\*; vt\_D = J + c\_B + u·g\_M, and D fires ⟺ g\_M = 1; under blockade u = 0 and vt\_D = J + c\_B \< θ\_D. ∎  
　Reading: whether the old and new Trace-Responses belong to one organization depends on KC overlap and the return wiring; this is an instance, on the neural side, of the “organizational transformations of the whole, of subsets and of their mutual intervention” of the ontological explanatory note. Within Type I, “the impediment is lifted after blockade, which supports the return mechanism” does not hold: the blockade makes the second write not occur at all.  
4.4  Step 4  Historical Dynamics Forms Tendency  
4.4.1  What Bears Tendency \[verified Q4c–Q4e\]  
　Tendency (D18-6 of \[18\]) is the inclination to activity that the organization of the set forms under the declared background, residues and input class, Φ\_σ: 𝒮 → 𝒴, defined on labeled signals; activity is the value of tendency at the actual signal. Neural side: the existing Trace-Responses T and their organization give the same odor different MBON drive and DAN recruitment under different training histories—this is precisely a difference in inclination, route, ease or choice. The signals whose tendency differs before and after training are exactly CS+ and the odors overlapping CS+; for non-overlapping odors it is the same (Q4d).  
　Tendency received at the write position (D18-13 of \[18\]): with the rest of the complete state fixed, ψ\_x is the local activity read by the write at position x, as a function of the depth of x. Two kinds on the neural side:  
　(A) Within-compartment self-loop: the write at x ∈ K(s)×{c} reads DAN\_c, DAN\_c reads MBON\_c through the return U\_cc, and MBON\_c reads the depth at x’s own position—ψ\_x depends on the own-position depth, and the loop gain is given by the product of the slopes of R\_c, Q\_c, G\_c and U\_cc.  
　(B) Cross-compartment: the write at the α3 positions reads PPL1-α3, PPL1-α3 reads MBON-γ1pedc through U\_{γ1,α3}, and the latter reads the depth of the γ1 subset—the own-position depth of the α3 positions does not enter ψ\_x (the activity-mediated loop gain is 0), and the fate of α3 is determined by the tendency of another compartment (Q4c: as the α3 depth sweeps over 17 grid points, PPL1-α3(A) takes only one value). The direction of the long trace's write is borne by the short trace, not by the long trace itself.  
　Reading: as a depression-type Trace-Response deepens, it has two different signed read-outs—(1) weakened transmission in its own channel: the MBON drive to CS+ decreases; (2) stronger disinhibition of the downstream DAN: through inhibitory return, DAN recruitment by CS+ rises (Q4e: the MBON-γ1 drive falls from 2 to 1/2, PPL1-α3 rises from −3/4 to 3/4). “The deeper receives the flow first” holds only when (2) is declared as the read-out bearing tendency, and only for that branch of return (MVP2 → PPL1-α3) and its operating domain; it cannot be used to issue, in their place, the ordering of the own channel or of the whole network.  
Theorem 19-4 (graded sign law: the direction of read-out-position Trace-Responses through modulatory return) \[proved; verified Q4a, Q4b\]  
　In a member of 𝒩₃F, let the return coefficient of compartment c be U\_cc = u, and let the MBONs of the other compartments not read the positions of compartment c. For one signal s (a\_i(s) ≥ 0) and two states d ≤ d′ (differing only at positions of compartment c, position-wise nondecreasing), the write component W\_x at x ∈ compartment c with a\_x(s) \> 0 satisfies:  
　(1) Depression type: u \> 0 ⇒ W\_x(d′) ≤ W\_x(d) (self-limiting); u \< 0 ⇒ W\_x(d′) ≥ W\_x(d) (continued writing); u = 0 ⇒ equality. “Continued writing/self-limiting” refers only to the direction of the write component with depth; actual net deepening is given by Theorem 19-2, and protection by Theorem 19-5.  
　(2) Potentiation type: the directions are the opposite of (1).  
　(3) Strictness: if u ≠ 0, η\_c a\_x \> 0, and the composite map actually changes between the actual inputs of the two states (the increments of R\_c between the two MBON inputs, of Q\_c between the two DAN inputs and of G\_c between the two DAN values are all nonzero), the inequality is strict; when the composite difference is zero, there is zero change. Strictness must be decided by the composite difference over the actual input interval.  
　Proof: for the depression type, MBON\_c = R\_c(Σ a\_i w⁰(1 − d\_i)/w⁰) is monotonically nonincreasing in the depth of each position of compartment c; DAN\_c = Q\_c(β\_c + US + u·MBON\_c + Σ\_{c′≠c} U\_{c′c}MBON\_{c′}), the other MBON\_{c′} being unchanged, so the direction of DAN\_c in MBON\_c is sgn(u); G\_c is nondecreasing and η\_c a\_x ≥ 0. The direction of a composition of monotone functions is the product of the directions: (−)·sgn(u)·(+) = −sgn(u). For the potentiation type, MBON\_c is monotonically nondecreasing and the direction is +sgn(u). Strictness: when u ≠ 0, the increments of every stage between the actual inputs are nonzero with definite direction, so the composite increment is nonzero. ∎  
　Status: an instance on 𝒩₃F of Theorem 18-5(3) of \[18\] (direction law: m(ψ\_x)·m(w\_x)), with the monotone direction of ψ\_x = read-out orientation × return sign. It covers every monotone chain “read-out-position Trace-Response → read-out → modulation → write at the same position”, whether or not the thresholds are binary. Q4a: over 600 pairs of random members and depth pairs, 0 have the opposite direction, each of the four cells has a strict witness, and 208 lie on plateaus.  
Corollary 19-4a (binary sign law) \[proved; verified Q4b\]  
　On the two orientations of 𝒦\_C, assume the probe-switch antecedent (there are a shallow and a deep state for which M's firing differs on an injection-free CS+ probe beat) and the DAN-switch antecedent (for u \> 0, c\_A \< θ\_D ≤ c\_A + u; for u \< 0, c\_A + u \< θ\_D ≤ c\_A). Then D on the probe beat “fires when deep and not when shallow” if and only if (potentiation type and u \> 0) or (depression type and u \< 0).  
　Proof: under the DAN-switch antecedent, g\_D = g\_M for u \> 0 and g\_D = 1 − g\_M for u \< 0; combining these with the two orientations of the probe switch, the four cells are read off one by one. ∎ Q4b: the directions of the LOOP model of \[18\] (u = −1 continued writing, u = 1/2 self-limiting) and of the binary members Φ\_sr and Φ\_α^I agree with Theorem 19-4—the binary, graded and upstream-model realizations all point the same way.  
Theorem 19-5 (depression-type Trace-Responses on an inhibitory self-loop: use-dependent protection) \[proved; verified Q8a, Q9h, Q11p\]  
　Setting: 𝒦\_C of Lemma 19-2a. Antecedents P1: u \< 0 and c\_A + u \< θ\_D ≤ c\_A; P2: dm \> 0; P3: ε ≤ s\* \< hi; P4: 0 ≤ ρ \< 1; P5: A₋ \> ε. Write L := min(hi, (1 − ρ)s\* + dm), K\_P(m): (1 − ρ)^m·L \> s\*, and f\_m(s) := (1 − ρ)^m·min(hi, (1 − ρ)s + dm).  
　(1) Continued writing: when the pre-beat s \> s\* on a CS+ beat, M does not fire and D fires (for any I\_D ≥ 0), the MINUS write component is +A₋ in depth (belonging to Act^ε), and the post-beat s′ ≥ L. Three things are kept apart here: a positive write component (+A₋ \> ε); maintenance (s′ ≥ L, and, when dm \> ρs\* (equivalently K\_P(0)), L \> s\*, so s′ stays in the continued-writing region); effective deepening (s′ \> s, which additionally requires s \< hi and dm \> ρs). The first two do not imply the third.  
　(2) Protection: when K\_P(m) holds and s \> s\* before some CS+ beat t₀, then for any subsequent input history in which the number of non-CS+ beats between consecutive CS+ beats is always ≤ m (injections arbitrary and nonnegative), every subsequent CS+ beat has s \> s\* and D fires.  
　(3) Same-beat certificate: at any injection-free continued-writing CS+ beat t after t₀, the MINUS event at x ∈ A×{M} has an effective-difference witness in the sense of Definition 6′ of \[17\]: (A) same beat, same process, same target; (B) the control sets only T = A×{M} back to baseline, and these positions have pre-beat depth s \> s\* ≥ ε and formation and retention records, from the zero initial state, earlier than t; (C) under the control M fires, D's input is c\_A + u \< θ\_D, D does not fire, and the MINUS component changes from −A₋ to 0, \|Δ\_tot\| = A₋ \> ε; (D) the route is given by the model structure (model mode), and the same-beat component is \|−A₋\| \> ε. Along the order “first swap the activity, then change the direct read”, A = Δ\_tot and D = 0.  
　(4) Periodic protocol: with ρ \> 0, the input a periodic sequence of “CS+ followed by exactly m rest beats”, no injection, and s \> s\* at the starting CS+ beat: if f\_m(s*) \> s*, every CS+ beat continues writing; if f\_m(s*) \< s*, after finitely many periods s ≤ s*, and there is no continued writing thereafter; f\_m(s*) = s\* is not issued.  
　(5) On the segments without CS+ there is only relaxation, and s decays as (1 − ρ)^k.  
　Proof: (1) By Lemma 19-2a(1)(2), when s \> s*, vt\_D = c\_A + I\_D ≥ c\_A ≥ θ\_D; by Lemma 19-2a(3) and dm \> 0, s′ = min(hi, (1 − ρ)s + dm) ≥ L (min and the affine term are both monotonically nondecreasing). (2) Induction on the sequence of CS+ beats after t₀: after the beat s ≥ L; the following k ≤ m non-CS+ beats only relax, so before the next CS+ beat s ≥ (1 − ρ)^k L ≥ (1 − ρ)^m L \> s*. (3) After setting back to baseline, s = 0 ≤ s*, M fires, vt\_D = c\_A + u \< θ\_D (P1, no injection), D does not fire, and MINUS = −A₋·y′\_D = 0; actually it is −A₋, and P5 gives \|Δ\_tot\| \> ε and same-beat action; clause (B) is given by (2), s \> s* ≥ ε (P3), together with the threshold-crossing record from the zero initial state. (4) f\_m is nondecreasing and continuous, and for ρ \> 0 its Lipschitz constant is ≤ (1 − ρ)^{m+1} \< 1, so g(s) := f\_m(s) − s is strictly decreasing and has a unique fixed point s\_f; if g(s*) \> 0, then s \> s* ⇒ f\_m(s) ≥ f\_m(s*) \> s*, and induction gives continued writing throughout; if g(s*) \< 0, then s\_f \< s*, s\_n − s\_f ≤ q^n(s\_0 − s\_f) → 0 with q := (1 − ρ)^{m+1}, after finitely many steps s\_n ≤ s\*, and thereafter M fires, D does not fire, and s only decays. (5) Lemma 19-2a(3). ∎  
　Verification: Q8a on Φ\_sr (s\* = 1/8, L = 15/64, K\_P(m) holding for m = 1–4): over 2880 beats of random input histories, 0 disagreements with (1) and (2); under the periodic protocol, m = 2 continues writing throughout and m = 6 ends continued writing.  
　Remarks: a positive net component (dm \> ρs) is kept apart from effective deepening (which additionally needs s \< hi); at full depth the positive net component is cut off by the upper clip (example X17, Q11p); (2) asserts only that s stays above s\*, not that it increases monotonically. Protection is use-dependent (writing continues only on re-exposure), and by Theorem 19-4 it exists only in the cell “depression type × inhibitory return”. For the continued-writing criterion in graded form see P19-8 (η·DAN(s) \> ρ·s, Q9h).  
Theorem 19-6 (depression-type Trace-Responses on an excitatory self-loop: no protection, self-limiting and signed read-out) \[proved; verified Q8b\]  
　Setting: 𝒦\_C of Lemma 19-2a; antecedent Q1: u \> 0 and c\_A + u \< θ\_D; P2–P5 as in Theorem 19-5; training injects J into D on CS+ beats.  
　(1) No protection: on any injection-free CS+ beat D does not fire and s′ = (1 − ρ)s; unpaired re-exposure after training does not write T. (2) Type I (J + c\_A \< θ\_D ≤ J + c\_A + u, teaching alone insufficient): a reinforced CS+ beat writes ⟺ s ≤ s*; over the whole history s ≤ s\_lim := min(hi, (1 − ρ)s* + dm). (3) Signed read-out: on an injection-free CS+ beat vt\_D = c\_A + u·\[s ≤ s*\], so after training (s \> s*) the drive of D to CS+ is lower than untrained by u. (4) Type II (J + c\_A ≥ θ\_D): every reinforced CS+ beat writes; there is no self-limiting. (5) Blockade of MBON→DAN during training (u ← 0): Type I training writes nothing, and the end state of Type II training is unchanged. (6) Same-beat certificate (the self-limiting dependence of Type I): on a reinforced CS+ beat t with ε \< s ≤ s\* (M and D both fire, MINUS = −A₋), the control sets T to a value deeper than s\* (≤ hi), so that M does not fire, D's input is J + c\_A \< θ\_D, and MINUS becomes 0, \|Δ\_tot\| = A₋ \> ε; the remaining clauses are as in Theorem 19-5(3); the two branches are A = Δ\_tot, D = 0. Issuing additionally requires: that control value belongs to the declared family of legitimate joint settings (counterfactual legitimacy, Definition 6′ of \[17\]); every position of T has earlier formation and retention records before t (s \> ε formed by previous reinforced CS+ beats and retained until t); the first reinforced beat from the zero initial state (s = 0) is not issued. The positive write component A₋ is still kept apart from effective deepening: the latter additionally requires s \< hi and dm \> ρs.  
　Proof: (1) vt\_D = c\_A + u·g\_M ≤ c\_A + u \< θ\_D. (2) When s ≤ s*, M fires, vt\_D = J + c\_A + u ≥ θ\_D, the write occurs, and s′ = min(hi, (1 − ρ)s + dm) ≤ s\_lim; when s \> s*, vt\_D = J + c\_A \< θ\_D, there is no write, and s′ ≤ s; induction from 0 ≤ s\_lim. (3) Lemma 19-2a(2). (4) vt\_D ≥ J + c\_A ≥ θ\_D. (5) Under Type I, vt\_D = J + c\_A \< θ\_D and D never fires; under Type II, D fires under both laws and the trajectories are the same. (6) Direct computation. ∎  
　Verification: Q8b on Φ\_α (s\* = 1/8, s\_lim = 63/256): 0 disagreements with (1), (2), (4) and the whole-history bound.  
　Reading: if α1 is an excitatory return (MBON-α1 is glutamatergic, and NMDA receptor knockdown in PAM-α1 impairs long-term memory, per the results of F7), re-exposure within the compartment provides no protection; long-term retention must be borne by something else (very slow relaxation, law-layer consolidation or activity in the odor-free period); see 4.5.3 and 4.6.5.  
4.5  Step 5  Tendency Carried Out as Activity  
4.5.1  Recruitment, Passage, Activation and Inactivation  
　Activity y = Φ\_σ(s) (D18-7 of \[18\]): at actual presentation, which KCs fire (determined by the odor and the KC adaptation residue), which MBONs cross threshold or at what level they fire (determined by the placement of Trace-Responses and the KC subset), which DANs are recruited (determined jointly by MBON→DAN return and the reinforcement signal), and behavioral approach and avoidance (a read-out of the MBON outputs of several compartments). External signals (odor, reinforcement) and internal continuation (KC adaptation, spontaneous activity in the odor-free period, persistent activity in return loops) take part together.  
　\[Fact\] The responses of PPL1-α3 and PPL1-α′2α2 to CS+ turn from a decrease to an increase during training and return to a decrease after 1 h; the evoked response of PPL1-γ1pedc to CS+ falls relative to baseline (F3: Fig. 3e, Extended Data Fig. 5a, attractive odor). Reading: same odor, different training histories, DAN recruitment of opposite sign—this is a reading of “the existing set constrains current activity”.  
4.5.2  The Probe Is a Write \[verified Q5a, Q5b\]  
　Theorem 18-3(4) of \[18\]: when tendency is read with s while the write gate is open, d changes accordingly. In the verified model (FLY of \[18\]), when tendency is read with CS+ after training, the K(A) positions of α3 enter Act^ε and their post-state changes (Q5a); when α3 is already at the saturation end, the same probe has a nonzero component but an unchanged post-state, and by the actual post-state it is non-invasive (Q5b). These two conclusions belong only to the verified model. Reading: the 24 h reading of F2 is a group mean with intermediate tests; whether these tests actually wrote α3 in the experiment is a conditional inference—if T2 and H3 hold under that protocol, they are write events (claim T5, P19-5); for delayed readings with training only and no intermediate tests, see P19-14.  
4.5.3  The Odor-Free Period and Internal Continuation; Three Kinds of “Cannot Express”  
Theorem 19-11 (internal continuation in the odor-free period) \[proved; verified Q5c, Q10q, Q11m, Q11n\]  
　(1) In 𝒦\_C (λ\_M = 0, I\_M = 0, no KC activation), the MBON does not fire on odor-free beats, and the return has no action on T. In 𝒩₃F, if the in-window MBON input reads no position whose depth changes (a\_i(bg) = 0 at every position where the depths of the two states differ), then the in-window MBON reading is independent of T. An external MBON drive that does not depend on depth can raise the baseline or open the DAN gate, but it brings no history condition (Q10q: an external drive with truncation threshold −1/2 holds the in-window MBON at 1/2, and the write does not read T).  
　(2) Suppose that (A) the weighted sum of background KC activity over the depth changes is nonzero (Σ\_i a\_i(bg)(d\_i − d′\_i) ≠ 0; this holds automatically when the two states change position by position in the same direction and some changed position has a\_i(bg) \> 0; when the two states change with opposite signs, the mere existence of an active changed position does not imply a change in the weighted sum), (B) the write target has a\_x(bg) \> 0, (C) R and Q both vary between the actual inputs of the two states (the return and the gate can pass), and (D) the total component difference exceeds ε. Then the two-state component difference of the in-window write W\_x = η a\_x(bg) G(DAN\_c) is borne by the activity-mediated branch (A = Δ\_tot, D = 0), with its direction fixed by Theorem 19-4. This item issues only the “activity-mediated component difference”.  
　(3) Issuing, in addition, the effective-difference certificate of Definition 6′ of \[17\], or HDyn, further requires: same-beat actual action \|W\_x\| \> ε (the actual arm itself exceeds ε); a control belonging to the declared legitimate joint-setting family; bearing positions already left before that beat (Definition 2′) and belonging to the same T; and a route certified by the mechanism (in model mode, given by the structure). Example X16 (Q11m): the background write has actual W = 0 and control W = −1/4, with A = −1/4, D = 0—all four conditions of (2) hold, yet the actual action does not exceed ε, so no certificate is issued; for an example with changes of opposite sign, see Q11n.  
　Proof: (1) MBON\_c = R\_c(Σ a\_i(bg)(1 − d\_i) + external term), which is independent of d when it reads no changed position. (2) (A) makes Σ a\_i(bg)(1 − d\_i) differ between the two states; (C) makes the composite difference nonzero; (B) makes the write exist; (D) gives a two-state total component difference exceeding ε; the order “first swap the activity, then change the direct read” of \[17\] assigns the branches as A = Δ\_tot, D = 0. (3) The clauses of Definition 6′ require, item by item, actual action, a legitimate control, bearing positions already left, and a route; (2) supplies neither the actual action nor the prior leaving. ∎  
　Verification: in a member with inhibitory return, with two beats of background signal after training: without background activity, the witness process of the Definition 6′ certificate is relaxation and the activity-mediated positive component ledger SED\_W is absent; with background activity of 1/2 per KC, the witness process is the write W and SED\_W holds (Q5c).  
　The representational limits of the binary class fall into three kinds:  
　(A) Model choice too narrow (expressible with the existing upstream classes and those of this paper): grading of MBON output, and activation stronger than natural levels (the thermogenetic activation of F7)—𝒩₃F is a graded class, and Definition 6′ of \[17\] and the runs and read-back of \[18\] hold for any declared host; thermogenetic-type external activation is, in 𝒩₃F, an exogenous drive to the MBON. The action of the return in the odor-free period is given directly in 𝒩₃F by Theorem 19-11(2).  
　(B) Existing classes not yet used (representation already provided upstream): a consolidation state that changes with DAN activity or with time after training—the law-layer component D18-11 and Theorem 18-8 of \[18\] already represent “the write law rewritten by history”, and FLYC of \[18\] gives a history-controlled and a clock-controlled version, which are executable witnesses (Q6h). The order of KC and DAN within one stimulus slot determines the direction of the write—the residue r (eligibility trace) and the fine beats of \[18\] provide a representational interface; a concrete implementation awaits construction (U19-3).  
　(C) Extensions not yet closed: continuous time and stochastic read-out, registered under U18-1 of \[18\] (U19-4). The representational gap: continuous-time flow and stochastic read-out laws are not declared in D18-1. The adjudicative gap: U9 of \[S11\], U13-19 of \[S13\] and HC^c of Definition 9′ of \[17\] are not adopted. The graded class used here is discrete-beat and deterministic.  
Note 19-8 (issuing conditions for bypass rescue) \[proved; verified Q10r, Q11o\]  
　That the write proceeds as usual after “preventing MBON firing and adding external DAN activation” shows only that the downstream segment suffices to support the write. Only when the mediator is genuinely fixed (DAN gating takes the same value under every legitimate setting, as when, in a binary member, the injection makes vt\_D ≥ θ\_D hold for all σ) and the bypass budget qualifies (the side-effect bounds of the injection and of MBON silencing have been declared) is it issued that the difference on T of the event through DAN at that beat is zero. In a graded member the external injection only shifts the DAN input and does not fix the mediator; the write difference on T is unchanged (Q10r: 1/4 against 1/4), and zero difference is not issued. “The write difference is unchanged in the graded case” holds only when the DAN inputs under both states and both injection conditions fall within the same slope interval of Q and G; across intervals the difference can change (Q11o: 1/16 with the control state on the lower DAN plateau, 1/4 after an injection of +2). When the bypass side-effect bound b\_byp is nonzero, only the total difference \|Δ\_tot\| ≤ b\_byp is issued; the component difference of the event through the fixed DAN can be exactly zero, but issuing “the difference on T is zero” further requires that the corresponding directly read positions (the direct-read branch) stay unchanged across the two states.  
　Proof: in the binary case g\_D is identically 1 for all σ, and y′\_D is independent of σ; in the graded case within one slope interval, W = η·k·(β + Δβ + U·m(σ)) + constant, and the two-state difference η·k·U·(m(σ) − m(σ′)) is independent of Δβ. ∎  
4.6  Step 6  Activity Acts Again on the Set  
4.6.1  The Mechanism Chain in a Single Declared Instance  
　The readings used each belong to different groups of animals or different protocols, and they do not form a per-animal, same-beat measured chain:  
　(A) During training (three rounds of CS+ paired with electric shock) and after training, the response of PPL1-α3 to CS+ goes from decrease to increase and returns to decrease after 1 h (voltage imaging) \[Fact\] F3.  
　(B) Blockade of MVP2 transmission during conditioning (Fig. 4i–j, n = 12 per group): the training-dependent response of PPL1-α3 vanishes \[Fact\] F3. This blockade was applied during conditioning; it is not a window-specific blockade at re-exposure in Fig. 5j.  
　(C) Extinction protocol (F4): Fig. 5j compares, at the 3 h endpoint, groups split by innate odor valence and by the two extinction onset times (10 min, 2 h); the extinction effect depends on valence and time point; Fig. 5k separately shows an early enhancement at 35 min for attractive odors \[Fact\]. The specific between-bar effects of 5j (groups, controls and the corresponding results) are not cited as evidence; its support for T2 extends only to “the action of re-exposure on α3 varies with the time after training”, and the specific assignment of directions does not rest on F4.  
　(D) The write at the KC→MBON-α3 synapse on re-exposure beats \[Mechanism hypothesis\] (not measured).  
　(E) The 24 h reading of MBON-α3 includes intermediate tests \[Fact\] F2.  
　Composition of the mechanism chain: (A) and (B) support “γ1 Trace-Response → MVP2 → PPL1-α3”; (C) supports “the action of re-exposure on α3 varies with the time after training”; (D) is the theoretical hypotheses H1 and H3. By Definition 10′ of \[17\] this is a cross-record chain within one paper: C1 (the same cell type, defined by the driver line) holds; C4′ requires a proof of correspondence of the mediator state at write time (the actual state of PPL1-α3 on the re-exposure beat corresponding to the state measured in link one), which the comparison of the two groups at 10 min and 2 h cannot issue on its own; C2, C3′ and C6 are not established (4.6.4). The original figures mark single-fly points. The only data relied on are data\_and\_parameters/Imaging\_24hr\_data.xlsx (group means and SEM of the 24 h imaging) in the original’s accompanying model repository (schnitzer-lab/Luo\_Huang\_2024\_MB\_model), together with the parameter files; the original has further public channels: the figure-by-figure Source Data listed under Data availability, and the Zenodo record 10.5281/zenodo.10998457 (neural voltage-activity traces and behavioral data for the five main figures). These records have not yet been compiled animal by animal under the declared criterion and recomputed (F11, U19-6).  
　In the declared model this chain closes: training writes γ1 and α3; re-exposure at 10 min disinhibits PPL1-α3 through the γ1 Trace-Response and writes K(CS+)×{α3}; re-exposure at 2 h, when γ1 has already shallowed, weakens α3; the rewritten α3 determines the MBON-α3 drive of the next CS+.  
　This chain is the junction, within a single declared instance, of “existing structure affects activity” (γ1 Trace-Response → reduced MBON-γ1pedc drive → disinhibition of PPL1-α3) and “activity changes structure” (PPL1-α3 → α3 write). The MBON→DAN return is one bearer within it, and the following bearers take part as well: the natural recovery of γ1 (the direct-read branch, which fixes the time window of the chain), KC overlap (which fixes the affected subset), out-of-boundary reads such as PPL1-α′2α2 (which fix in-boundary status), and the consolidation state (which fixes the long-term retention of α3).

### 4.6.2  Cross-Compartment Gating

Theorem 19-7 (cross-compartment: the short trace gates the long-trace write) \[proved; verified Q8c, Q11s, Q11t\]  
　Working class 𝒦\_X: topological order M1 ≺ D1, M1 ≺ D2, M1 ≺ D2m; gp(M1) = Z; λ = 0, d = 0, v\_reset = 0; ρ₁ (M1 column), ρ₂ (M2 column); baseline hi for the M1 and M2 columns; the D columns are static drives c₁, c₂, c\_m (per KC); C0; training injects J into D1 on CS+ beats (I\_D2 = I\_D2m = I\_M1 = 0). Write s₁, s₂ for the common depths of T₁, T₂ on A. Action threshold s₁\* := (n\_A·hi − θ\_M1)/n\_A: M1 fires to CS+ ⟺ s₁ ≤ s₁*. “MVP2 recovers” means that s₁ returns to ≤ s₁*, that is, M1 fires to CS+ again.  
　Antecedents: X1 (disinhibition is threshold crossing) c₂\_A ≥ θ\_D2 \> c₂\_A + U₁₂; X2 (unpaired CS+ does not write T₁) c₁\_A \< θ\_D1; X3 (signed members only) c\_m,A + U\_m ≥ θ\_m \> c\_m,A.  
　(1) On a CS+ beat without injection, g\_D2 = \[s₁ \> s₁*\]; for signed members, g\_D2m = \[s₁ ≤ s₁*\].  
　(2) Unpaired CS+ beat: T₁ only relaxes. When s₁ \> s₁*, D2 fires and MINUS\_{i,α3} = −A₋, with the three-way split as in Theorem 19-2(4); when s₁ ≤ s₁*, binary members do not write, and for signed members s₂′ = max(0, (1 − ρ₂)s₂ − A₊); for s₂ \> 0, strict weakening ⟺ A₊ \> 0 or ρ₂ \> 0 (when A₊ = ρ₂ = 0, s₂′ = s₂, Q11t).  
　(3) Let the T₁ depth at the end of training be σ, with re-exposure after k rest beats. k\* := min{k ≥ 0 : σ(1 − ρ₁)^k ≤ s₁*} (∞ for the empty set). Under 0 ≤ s₁* \< hi, k\* \< ∞ ⟺ σ ≤ s₁\* ∨ ρ₁ = 1 ∨ (ρ₁ \> 0 ∧ s₁\* \> 0); the degenerate branches are treated separately: when s₁\* \< 0 (M1 never fires to CS+, s₁ ≥ 0 \> s₁*), k* = ∞; when s₁\* ≥ hi (M1 always fires), k\* = 0 (Q11s). MVP2 fires to that CS+ ⟺ k ≥ k*; when k* \< ∞, the flip delay of the α3 effect and the recovery delay of MVP2 are both k*; when k* = ∞, the flip never occurs.  
　(4) If during training an injection ≥ θ\_M1 is made into M1 on every reinforced CS+ beat, then, from the zero initial state, T₂ = 0 at the end of training.  
　(5) Blockade U₁₂, U\_m ← 0: g\_D2 = \[c₂\_A ≥ θ\_D2\] = 1 and g\_D2m = \[c\_m,A ≥ θ\_m\] = 0 on every CS+ beat, and the late potentiation write component (the weakening write) vanishes; when ρ₂ \> 0, relaxation still lowers s₂, so “the late weakening vanishes” refers only to the write component, not to s₂ failing to decrease (Q11t).  
　Proof: (1) M1 fires to CS+ ⟺ n\_A(hi − s₁) ≥ θ\_M1 ⟺ s₁ ≤ s₁*; vt\_D2 = c₂\_A + U₁₂·g\_M1, and X1 gives g\_D2 = 1 − g\_M1; vt\_D2m = c\_m,A + U\_m·g\_M1, and X3 gives g\_D2m = g\_M1. (2) The input to D1 is ≤ c₁\_A \< θ\_D1 (X2), the MINUS\_{i,M1} component is 0, and T₁ only relaxes; the M2 column is computed with the gating of (1) in the same way as Lemma 19-2a(3) (under potentiation hi − S = (1 − ρ₂)s₂ − A₊, with lower clip 0). (3) Rest beats have no KC activation, every unit input is 0 or U·0, and there is only relaxation; before the re-exposure beat s₁ = σ(1 − ρ₁)^k and s₂ = (1 − ρ₂)^k s₂,end; substitute into (1) and (2). Extended values: when σ ≤ s₁*, k = 0 already suffices; when ρ₁ = 1, k = 1 gives 0 ≤ s₁*; when ρ₁ ∈ (0, 1) and s₁* \> 0, σ(1 − ρ₁)^k → 0; when ρ₁ = 0 and σ \> s₁*, the depth stays \> s₁*; when ρ₁ ∈ (0, 1), s₁\* = 0 and σ \> 0, it stays \> 0. (4) g\_M1 = 1, vt\_D2 = c₂\_A + U₁₂ \< θ\_D2, and the MINUS\_{i,M2} component is 0. (5) Substitution. ∎  
　Verification: Q8c on Φ\_γα3^0 (s₁\* = 1/8), over 5 values of ρ₁ × 3 values of σ × 40 rest lengths, finds 0 violations of “MVP2 fires ⟺ k ≥ k\*” and of “g\_PPL1-α3 = 1 − g\_MVP2”.  
Theorem 19-7′ (graded cross-compartment gating) \[proved; verified Q6c, Q6d, Q6e, Q9a, Q9b, Q10k, Q10l, Q10m, Q11f–Q11i\]  
　Consider a two-compartment member of 𝒩₃F with compartment γ (short trace, ρ\_γ ≥ 0) and compartment α (long trace, ρ\_α = 0), U\_{γα} = U \< 0, all other returns 0, η\_α \> 0, a\_i(CS+) = 1 on K (\|K\| = n\_A), and re-exposure beats without reinforcement. Write s for the common depth of γ on K, and m\_γ := R(n\_A(1 − s) − θ\_γ).  
　(1) Slope of the same-beat component: if m\_γ \> 0 and β\_α + U·m\_γ \> 0 (linear regime), the α-position write W = η\_α·G(β\_α + U·m\_γ) satisfies ∂W/∂s = η\_α·\|U\|·n\_A (G is the identity or z − θ\*, with slope 1); over the whole domain W is monotonically nondecreasing in s. A late reading (such as the MBON reading at 3 h) also contains the subsequent evolution and the read-out map, and the matched no-re-exposure arm must be subtracted from it: when the two arms have identical training and the same α state before re-exposure, and α has no relaxation and is not clipped, the difference in the final α states is exactly W (Q10m). Training strength itself changes the trained end state of α (Q10m: α end states 3/64, 5/32, 5/16 after 1, 2, 3 rounds of training), so a raw late reading cannot stand in for the same-beat slope. In general, late-reading difference = read-out conversion ∘ difference propagation: under a common linear rest decay r\_j, the write W\_s of re-exposure beat s propagates to the read-out as W\_s·∏\_{j\>s} r\_j, and when the read-out is the MBON drive it is further multiplied by the read-out slope n\_A (linear regime); claiming the same slope at the 3 h MBON or behavioral layer requires the declared maps of both segments (read-out assumption RO1, P19-1).  
　(2) First beats (signed write, zero point θ\* \> 0): W \> 0 ⟺ m\_γ \< m\* := (β\_α − θ*)/\|U\|; W = 0 ⟺ m\_γ = m* (zero write; the actual post-state additionally passes through clipping, see (3)); W \< 0 ⟺ m\_γ \> m*. Let the γ depth at the end of training be σ, with re-exposure after k rest beats; define (with extended values) k₊ := min{k : W(k) ≤ 0} (first time the write is no longer positive) and k₋ := min{k : W(k) \< 0} (first reverse write). Then k₊ = min{k : m\_γ(k) ≥ m*}, k₋ = min{k : m\_γ(k) \> m*} ≥ k₊, and W(k) \< 0 for k ≥ k₋. Separate cases: (A) first beat exactly zero: when m\_γ(k₊) = m*, k₋ \> k₊ (Q10l); (B) exactly at the zero point in the long run: when m\* equals the MBON upper bound n\_A − θ\_γ (s\_m = 0), W \> 0 holds throughout while tending to 0 from the positive side, and k₊ = ∞, with tail proof σ(1 − ρ\_γ)^k \> 0 (Q10l, Q11h); (C) ρ\_γ = 0 and m\_γ(σ) \< m*: k₊ = ∞, with the constancy of m\_γ as tail proof; ρ\_γ = 0 and m\_γ(σ) = m*: W is identically 0 (“permanently at the zero point”, k₊ = 0, k₋ = ∞), listed separately from (B), “tending to zero from the positive side”; (D) not seen within a finite window: only a lower bound is recorded, and ∞ is not issued. Tail-proof conditions for (B): σ \> 0, 0 \< ρ\_γ \< 1, and m\* a reachable positive upper bound of the MBON; when σ = 0, k₊ = 0, and when ρ\_γ = 1 the zero write is reached at beat 1.  
　(3) Actual deepening and weakening: when W \> 0 but α is at the upper end before the beat, there is no deepening (Example X6, Q10k: W = 1/16, α = 1 → 1); when W \< 0 but α is at the lower end before the beat, there is no weakening; strict weakening requires W \< 0 and a pre-beat α depth \> lo (Theorem 19-2(2)).  
　(4) Relation to the binary version: the flip threshold s₁\* of the binary version and m\* of the graded version are determined by different parameters, and they may coincide or fall within the same sampling interval; only when each is calibrated independently and the first-arrival intervals of the two candidates are separated in sampling does the flip time discriminate the binary reading from the graded one.  
　(5) Sampling intervals: suppose m\_γ(k) is monotonically nondecreasing over the sampling beats k = 0, 1, …, where m\_γ takes the pre-beat value of each re-exposure beat. For k₊ ≥ 1, m\_γ(k₊ − 1) \< m\* ≤ m\_γ(k₊); for k₋ ≥ 1, m\_γ(k₋ − 1) ≤ m\* \< m\_γ(k₋); together these give a bracketing interval for m*. k₊ = 0 (threshold already crossed at the first beat) gives only the one-sided bound m* ≤ m\_γ(0). Within the model, sampling a zero write exactly (W(k) = 0) means m\_γ(k) = m\* (a model identity); for experimental readings, the corresponding reading is issued as the threshold only with continuous time and transversality, and discrete sampling issues only a bracketing interval. Example X11 (Q11f): m\* = 1/2, with two consecutive beats (m, W) = (1/4, 1/8), (5/8, −1/16): k₊ = k₋ = 1, giving only 1/4 \< m\* \< 5/8; the zero point was not sampled.  
　Proof: (1) In the linear regime W = η\_α(β\_α + U(n\_A(1 − s) − θ\_γ)) − η\_α θ*·\[signed\]; differentiating in s gives η\_α\|U\|n\_A; the direction over the whole domain follows from Theorem 19-4 (cross-compartment form). Matched arm: thereafter both arms go through the same rest, α has no relaxation, and the difference stays W. (2) For η\_α \> 0, W has the sign of Q(β\_α + U·m\_γ) − θ*; for θ\* \> 0, Q(z) ≤ θ\* ⟺ z ≤ θ*, that is, m\_γ ≥ m*, and likewise for equality and strict inequality; rest relaxes only γ, so m\_γ is monotonically nondecreasing in k, the three first beats are defined by the corresponding inequalities, and for k ≥ k₋, m\_γ(k) ≥ m\_γ(k₋) \> m\*; (B) and (C) follow from the expression σ(1 − ρ\_γ)^k. (3) Theorem 19-2(2)(3). (4) Definition. (5) From (2) and the monotonicity of m\_γ. ∎  
Corollary 19-7″ (shift law) \[proved; verified Q9b, Q11g, Q11h\]  
　Assume a common threshold 0 \< s\_m \< σ, m\* \> 0, 0 \< ρ\_γ \< 1, no change of parameters between the two training strengths, c \> 1 and cσ ≤ 1; s\_m := 1 − (m\* + θ\_γ)/n\_A is the γ depth corresponding to zero write. Write q := ln(1/(1 − ρ\_γ)), x(σ) := ln(σ/s\_m)/q, L := ln c/q. Then k₊(σ) = ⌈x(σ)⌉, and k₊(cσ) − k₊(σ) ∈ {⌊L⌋, ⌈L⌉}. When L is an integer, this set reduces to {L}; when c = 2 and L is not an integer, it is {n₂ − 1, n₂}, n₂ := min{n : (1 − ρ\_γ)^n ≤ 1/2}. When L \< 1 (a shift of less than one beat), the difference can be 0, and equal delays remain consistent with the model (Example X12, Q11g: with ρ\_γ = 3/4 and s\_m = 1/4, σ = 1/2 and σ = 1 both first arrive at beat 1, L = 1/2); when σ ≤ s\_m, k₊ = 0, which gives only a one-sided bound. The continuous value t\_flip = τ\_γ1·ln(σ/s\_m) is issued only under common exponential shallowing, with common τ\_γ1 and s\_m.  
　Proof: k₊(σ) = min{k ≥ 0 : σ(1 − ρ\_γ)^k ≤ s\_m} = min{k : k ≥ x(σ)} = ⌈x(σ)⌉; x(cσ) = x(σ) + L, and for any real x and L ≥ 0, ⌈x + L⌉ − ⌈x⌉ ∈ {⌊L⌋, ⌈L⌉}. For c = 2, ⌈L⌉ = n₂. ∎  
　Status and reading: in the binary version (Theorem 19-7) the flip occurs when MVP2 crosses its action threshold s₁*; in the graded version it occurs when the MVP2 drive returns to m*, which is determined by the zero point of the learning rule and the strength of the return. On FLY of \[18\] (signed, PPL1-α3 = −a + bΣd\_γ1), the flip delay is a step function n\*(D) of the initial γ1 depth (PN-1 and T19 of \[18\]; Q6e). In the linear regime, the slope of the write in γ1 depth gives a graded dose–response (P19-1), distinguishable from the step of the binary version. Verification: among 300 members, the 228 cases in the linear regime show 0 violations of slope = η₂\|U₁₂\|n\_A (Q6c); the first flip beats show 0 violations (finite in 296 cases, not occurring within 200 beats in 4, including the never-flipping branch with ρ₁ = 0, Q6d).  
4.6.3  Read-Back: Evidence at the Duty Layer \[verified Q6a, Q6b, Q6f, Q6g\]  
　(1) Definition 6′ of \[17\] (model mode): in Fly(full), at rest beat 1 after training, the γ1 relaxation process Rg acts on g0, the control changes only g0, the total difference is 1/16 \> ε, and the two branches are A = 0, D = 1/16—this witness is borne by the direct-read branch (Q6a). By Definition 7′ of \[17\], Fld is given by any qualifying witness; for witnesses of the activity-mediated branch, see (3), (4) and Theorem 19-H.  
　(2) Chain certificate of Theorem 17-8′ of \[17\] (8.2.4 of \[17\]): at a delay of 2 beats, the effective difference is certified and the actual action is certified; at a delay of 6 beats, the effective difference is certified but the certified interval of the actual action intersects \[−ε, ε\] (⊥\_Act; shallowing does not exclude action); at a delay of 12 beats, the effective difference is not certified, ⊥\_Chain(C3′) (insufficient cancellation margin), while the actual action is certified (negative, an effective shallowing) (Q6b).  
　(3) Item-by-item certificates on the frozen contrast family of Theorem 18-4 of \[18\] (the α3 write of FLY of \[18\], linear law c = −ηa + ηbΣ\_{K(A)} d\_γ1, the contrast family consisting of the two single settings “set the γ1 position back to 0”): early on (γ1 depth 3/4), the complete certificate is 1, with summary “effective difference certified”; late (γ1 depth 1/64), the single-setting contrast difference is about 0.0039 ≤ ε, with summary “functional support established”, and HistCond\_F = 1—the late failure to certify is not read as absence of a history condition (Q6f).
