　(4) J₁₈ through the evaluator of \[S2\] (the three conditions of Theorem 18-4(4) of \[18\]): the event table consists of the out-of-boundary pulse of the training beat (forming the γ1 and α3 positions, tagged as out-of-boundary), the two α3 writes of the re-exposure beat (linear law, reading the two γ1 positions, coefficient ηb, bias −ηa) and the two γ1 relaxations; closure of the evidence graph is declared by the evidence protocol. D20 accepts; the original-mechanism trajectory and the F trajectory agree beat by beat; Trace\_F = Part\_F = HDyn\_F = Q\_F = 1, and both α3 write events have HistCond\_F = 1 (Q6g). This is one qualifying embedding of the cross-compartment write in the F layer of \[S2\]; it is a formal conclusion at the model layer, not a certification of material causation.  
4.6.4  Cross-Record Chains and Delayed Readings on the Real Object  
　(Columns: condition \| source and evidence layer \| status \| test given by the theory)  
　Link one (γ1 Trace-Response → PPL1-α3) \| F3 voltage imaging and transmission blockade \| two states = trained and untrained groups (across groups of animals), n = 12; r₁ awaits per-animal data \| the repository summary table used has only group means and SEM; the figure-by-figure Source Data and the Zenodo traces are available through other public channels (F11), but these records have not yet been compiled animal by animal under the declared criterion and recomputed, so column C cannot yet be recomputed per 4.3 of \[S3\].  
　Link two (PPL1-α3 → α3 write) \| F6 optogenetic drive (behavioral layer); F2 firing read-out \| law interval undetermined \| Theorem 19-7′(1) writes the required law as an estimable slope η₂\|U₁₂\|n\_A together with a linear regime, and P19-1 gives a dose–response design for estimating it.  
　C1  Node identity \| genetic driver lines define PPL1-α3 and α3 \| holds (declared) \| —  
　C2  Mediator fidelity \| optogenetic activation equivalent to physiological firing (class Cb of \[S13\]) \| not established \| the simultaneous-measurement design of P19-15 gives the optogenetic-to-physiological conversion ζ.  
　C3′  Law interval \| the PPL1-α3 firing in the two target states lies within the law interval of record two \| not established \| as above.  
　C4′  State correspondence \| the mediator state at the re-exposure write (the actual state of PPL1-α3) corresponds to the state measured in link one \| not established: the comparison of the two groups at 10 min and 2 h cannot issue C4′ on its own \| the same-trial dual-color imaging of P19-4 gives the mediator state at write time.  
　C5  Cut set \| blocking MVP2 removes the training dependence of PPL1-α3 \| cut-set evidence for link one; parallel reads such as PPL1-α′2α2 not excluded \| P19-4 (activating MVP2 during re-exposure rather than during training).  
　C6  Actual action \| the actual total component at the α3 target beat \| inferred from the firing layer via a map \[Mechanism hypothesis\]; bypass bound not given \| theoretical predictions in P19-4 and P19-6; the tests are preferred experiments ★1 and ★5.  
　Detection conditions for delayed readings \[verified Q6o\]: the training-period write of T₂ is proved at the model layer; the read-out after a delay of N beats is the reduction n\_A·(1 − ρ₂)^N·s\_end in the MBON-α3 drive to CS+. By Definition 2′ of \[17\], the self-retention of the reading relative to the detection threshold τ\_det is complete when a geometric envelope serves as tail evidence, and equals min{N : n\_A(1 − ρ₂)^N s\_end ≤ τ\_det}; if the reading is still detectable at the end of the record, only a lower bound is recorded and completeness is not issued (Q6o: 0 violations over 120 members, 11 with only a lower bound recorded). Hence “already written during training” does not imply “read at 24 h”; a failure to read it at 24 h excludes only the complete premise set (retention, map, detection power, training-period write) and does not by itself locate the failure in “not written during training”; reading it excludes only hypothesis Z16 (the frozen training and read-out hypothesis of the N5 model of \[S16\]: a reinforced passage writes nothing to the long trace), not other parallel-layer mechanisms (P19-14).  
4.6.5  Law-Layer Consolidation: The Write Law Rewritten by History \[verified Q6h–Q6m\]  
　How this paper uses Theorem 18-8(6) of \[18\]: under the same training, the same read-out and the same consolidation-window blockade, the consolidation state of the history-controlled arm is not set while that of the clock-controlled arm is set, and the final-beat readings of the two arms differ; without blockade both arms are set and cannot be discriminated (Q6h). Its antecedents (the two arms have the same initial complete state, apart from c, in everything relevant to the read-out, and the residue recursion does not read c) hold on FLYC; on the real object the “two arms” must be two groups of flies with the same training history, and the antecedent is that the residue states of the two groups before weak training are the same (the conditions carried by PC-1 and PC-2 of \[18\]).  
Theorem 19-10 (law-layer setting in the consolidation window: conditional results; the discrimination of in-window readings rests on Theorem 19-D as load-bearing) \[proved; verified Q6i–Q6m, Q9d, Q9e, Q10i, Q10j, Q11j, Q11k, Q11l, Q12c, Q12d, Q12e, Q12f, Q12g\]  
　Setting: Consol of Definition 19-1′. The control σ′ changes only depth positions already left in T (Definition 6′(B) of \[17\]). Construction labels: “K-set-deep” means that only the n\_A positions of CS+ are set to a common depth D, with the N − n\_A positions outside K at the ground state 0 (this construction is used in this theorem and in Q11j); “all-set-deep” means that all N positions are set to D (Example X5 uses this construction in Q10i: for D = 1, MBON = 0 and DAN\_w = κ). The two give different MBONs and are labeled separately.  
　(1) Constant branch (u·b = 0; in-window DAN independent of T): the in-window DAN is constantly Y₀ := Q(κ + u·R(−θ\_M)). The permissive type P refers specifically to u = 0, in which case Y₀ = Q(κ); b = 0 with u ≠ 0 is a return that does not read history, recorded separately as a constant branch, and its constant is in general not Q(κ) (Example X19, Q12c: b = 0, u = 1, θ\_M = −1, κ = 0, θ\_c = 1/2 give Y₀ = 1 ≥ θ\_c, so CONS sets when the tag is effective and the state not yet set, whereas judging the gate by Q(κ) = 0 would wrongly give no setting). In both cases the A branch of the total difference of CONS\_x is 0. If in addition Y₀ ≥ θ\_c and c\_x = 0 (the gate passes, settable), then when the control makes d\_x cross τ\_tag the D branch is −1, and the effective difference is borne by the direct-read branch; when Y₀ \< θ\_c, neither state sets and there is no difference (Q10j, Q12c). The constant-return branch does not satisfy the history-reading antecedent D1 of Theorem 19-D and is not merged into the guarantee domain of P.  
　(2) Return type (u·b ≠ 0): the direction of DAN\_w with respect to T is −sgn(u) (depression type; the in-window form of Theorem 19-4). Certificate across the DAN gate: along the order “first swap the activity, then change the direct read”, A = CONS(direct read of σ, y(σ′)) − CONS(direct read of σ, y(σ)), D = CONS(direct read of σ′, y(σ′)) − CONS(direct read of σ, y(σ′)). The A branch varies with the DAN gate only when the tag gate and the settable gate of the actual arm both hold (d\_x(σ) \> τ\_tag, c\_x = 0); when the tag gate of the actual arm fails, even a DAN gate crossing its threshold gives A = 0, and A is not issued. When the control switches the gate from closed to open (DAN\_w(σ) \< θ\_c ≤ DAN\_w(σ′)), the D branch after the direct read is swapped must also be checked: if σ′ drops d\_x below τ\_tag, D = −A and the total difference is 0 (Q11k). Under inhibitory return (u \< 0), the deeper the Trace-Response, the stronger the in-window DAN, and consolidation favors deep Trace-Responses; under excitatory return (u \> 0), the reverse holds.  
　(3) Between-arm difference of in-window DAN: its antecedents, exact value and resolution version are given in Theorem 19-D (load-bearing); the exact values are 0 for the permissive type and −u·Δb for the return type (Q9d).  
　(4) Setting set under excitatory return (K-set-deep, one beat, c\_x = 0, θ\_c \> 0, u \> 0, b \> 0, n\_A \> 0): write M(D) := R(b(N − n\_A·D) − θ\_M) and Y(D) := Q(κ + u·M(D)); Y is monotonically nonincreasing in D. DAN gate: when κ ≥ θ\_c it passes for every D; when κ \< θ\_c it passes ⟺ D ≤ D\_c := (κ + u·(b·N − θ\_M) − θ\_c)/(u·b·n\_A) (this formula is exact even on the plateaus of R and Q, because θ\_c \> 0 and u \> 0 put both stages on their linear branches whenever the gate passes). Setting set = {D ∈ \[0, 1\] : D \> τ\_tag} ∩ {DAN gate passes}. Hence “there is a depth whose tag is above threshold but which is not consolidated” ⟺ τ\_tag \< 1 and Y(1) \< θ\_c (within the declared domain where both stages are linear, Y(1) = κ + u·(b(N − n\_A) − θ\_M)); the interval of consolidated depths is (τ\_tag, min(1, D\_c)\] ((τ\_tag, 1\] when κ ≥ θ\_c), and it may be empty. Writing only the DAN gate while omitting the intersection with the tag gate and the settable gate, or omitting θ\_M and the non-plateau domain, gives wrong verdicts: Example X14 (Q11j), κ = 1, u = 1/2, θ\_c = 1/2, τ\_tag = 3/4: at D = 1/2 the DAN gate passes but the tag gate fails, so c′ = 0; Example X15 (Q11j), κ = b = 1/4, u = 1, N = 4, n\_A = 2, θ\_M = θ\_c = 1/2: at D = 1 the true DAN\_w = 1/4, while the lower-bound formula that omits θ\_M gives 3/4; Example X5, κ = 1, u = 1/2, b = 1/4, θ\_c = 1/2: κ ≥ θ\_c, so every depth passes the DAN gate (DAN\_w = 1 for all-set-deep D = 1, and 5/4 for K-set-deep; Q10i, Q11j). A monotone decrease does not imply a threshold crossing.  
　(5) Blocking DAN in the window (DAN\_w ← 0) prevents CONS, which requires θ\_c \> 0 (with θ\_c = 0 blockade still sets, Q10j); for blockade to weaken long-term retention, it is further required that the unblocked arm actually has CONS (all three gates hold), that the subsequent effective relaxation difference crosses the detection threshold within the read-out window, and that no other component cancels it. Under these three antecedents all three readings give “blockade impairs long-term retention”, so this observation does not discriminate among the three readings (Q6m).  
　(6) Retention with a window of L beats followed by N\_post rest beats (K-set-deep, with only relaxation and consolidation in the window): the first setting beat is t(D) := min{j \< L : all three gates hold at beat j}, which within the domain of (4) is t(D) = min{j \< L : τ\_tag \< D·ρ₀^j ≤ D\_c and c\_x still 0} (dropping the upper bound when κ ≥ θ\_c). Write H := L + N\_post; if setting occurs at beat t, h(D) := H − 1 − t; if no setting occurs, h(D) := 0. Then  
　　LTM(D) = D·ρ₀^{H−h(D)}·ρ₁^{h(D)};  
　and for D₁, D₂ \> 0 and 0 \< ρ₀ \< ρ₁ ≤ 1,  
　　LTM(D₂)/LTM(D₁) = (D₂/D₁)·(ρ₁/ρ₀)^{h(D₂)−h(D₁)}.  
　Within one t branch h is fixed, and LTM(D) = D·ρ₀^{H−h}·ρ₁^{h} is linear in D; for 0 \< ρ₀ \< ρ₁ ≤ 1 it is strictly increasing; for ρ₀ = 0 and H ≥ 1, the reading of this construction is identically 0 (Example X22, Q12g: ρ₀ = 0, ρ₁ = 9/10, τ\_tag = 1/4, D\_c = 1/2, L = 1, N\_post = 1; D = 3/8 and D = 1/2 are both set at beat 0, and both late readings are 0). The direction across a branch boundary is decided by this ratio formula and is not uniformly a drop: crossing the tag gate into the setting region (h going from 0 to positive) can produce a jump up; crossing the DAN upper limit and losing timely setting (h decreasing) can produce a jump down, and whether it does also depends on whether the depth ratio D₂/D₁ is smaller than (ρ₁/ρ₀)^{h(D₁)−h(D₂)}. A strictly visible jump down requires setting to occur before the read-out and to affect at least one relaxation beat (h ≥ 1). D is confined to \[0, 1\], and the tag gate cuts off the beats with D·ρ₀^j ≤ τ\_tag, so it is not the case that each additional window beat adds one more sawtooth (Q11l). When ρ₀ = 0, setting can occur at the first beat while the amount retained at the first beat is zero; all later readings are then zero, and the setting is invisible (Q12d). Example X20 (Q12d): ρ₀ = 1/2, ρ₁ = 9/10, τ\_tag = 1/4, D\_c = 1/2, L = 1, N\_post = 1; as D goes from 1/4 to 13/50, retention goes 1/16 → 117/1000 (jump up); as D goes from 1/2 to 51/100, retention goes 9/40 → 51/400 (jump down). The strict increase within branches and the jumps down across boundaries that Q12e finds on a finite grid of adjacent depths (generated domain 0 \< ρ₀ \< ρ₁ ≤ 1) belong to that grid only; for an arbitrary finite pair of depths the margin must be computed separately from the ratio formula—Example X21 (Q12f): with ρ₀ = 3/4, ρ₁ = 9/10, τ\_tag = 1/8, D\_c = 1/2, L = 2, N\_post = 1, D = 1/4 is set at beat 0 and D = 3/5 at beat 1 (later), yet the latter retains more.  
　Proof: (1) When u·b = 0, either b = 0 makes the MBON input −θ\_M, or u = 0 keeps the MBON out of DAN; both give DAN\_w = Q(κ + u·R(−θ\_M)) = Y₀, independent of d, so A = 0; the occurrence of CONS\_x reads d\_x \> τ\_tag, and when the control turns this from true to false, D = −1. (2) The MBON is nonincreasing in d, and the direction of DAN\_w in the MBON is sgn(u), so the composite direction is −sgn(u); A and D are defined in the stated order, with the direct read of σ in A, so the tag and settability take their values from the actual arm; in D the direct read is swapped to σ′. (4) Positions outside K are 0 and those in K are D, so the MBON input is b(N − n\_A D) − θ\_M. When κ ≥ θ\_c, Y ≥ Q(κ) = κ ≥ θ\_c. When κ \< θ\_c, Y(D) ≥ θ\_c \> 0 ⟺ κ + u·M(D) ≥ θ\_c ⟺ M(D) ≥ (θ\_c − κ)/u \> 0 ⟺ b(N − n\_A D) − θ\_M ≥ (θ\_c − κ)/u ⟺ D ≤ D\_c; then intersect with the tag gate and the settable gate. Existence: Y is nonincreasing, so an above-tag depth that is not consolidated exists ⟺ the DAN gate does not pass at D = 1 and 1 \> τ\_tag. (5) When θ\_c \> 0 = DAN\_w, CONS does not occur; the retention difference is the accumulated relaxation difference over the beats after the unblocked arm is set. (6) In each window beat the three gates are evaluated first and relaxation follows (the same beat reads the pre-beat depth); before setting the retention ratio is ρ₀, the setting beat itself still uses ρ₀, and ρ₁ applies thereafter, so there are H − h beats at ρ₀ and h beats at ρ₁ in all; the ratio for two depths follows by direct division. The in-window depth D·ρ₀^j decreases monotonically; the DAN gate passes when D·ρ₀^j ≤ D\_c, and the tag gate when D·ρ₀^j \> τ\_tag. ∎  
　Verification: permissive type A = 0, D = −1; inhibitory return A = −1, D = 0 (Q6i, Q6j); in-window DAN, trained group against sham-trained group: 1/2 against 1/2, 3/8 against 0, 5/16 against 1/2 (Q6k); with θ\_c = 3/8, only Trace-Responses with D ≤ 1/2 are consolidated (Q6l); in-window blockade reduces the depth after 22 beats under all three readings (Q6m); the complete equivalence for the setting set shows 0 violations over 14976 parameter × depth points (Q11j); the unified ratio formula shows 0 violations over 1152 depths (Q12f).  
　Comparison with material evidence (conditional reading): F7 (appetitive): blocking PAM-α1 output or MBON-α1 output for 1 h immediately after training impairs 24 h memory, while a blockade of the same length 22 h later does not. Output blockade is not a record of an in-window activity difference: blocking PAM-α1 output approximates “DAN output ← 0”, not “DAN activity ← 0”; manipulating MBON-α1 in the window does not realize DAN ← 0; under the antecedents of (5) the result is compatible with all three readings, and Fig. 9 cannot by itself adjudicate among the candidates of Theorem 19-D. It is a dependence reference for the early appetitive PAM-α1 window and is not transplanted directly as the same window for PPL1-α3. F8 (appetitive): it supports the GABAergic MVP2 → MP1 return and the regulation of MP1 oscillation; this paper's prediction about the direction of the in-window mean activity (cell R₋: trained group above sham-trained group) is listed separately as an item of P19-10 and is not issued by proxy from the change in oscillation. F9: trained and untrained (vehicle) groups recorded over 3 continuous hours, summarized by hour; the reported nonsignificance of the PAM-α1 readings is a comparison relative to baseline; nonsignificance is not taken as an equivalence verification of the permissive type; its control is not a stimulus-matched sham training, and D1–D4 and e, m, g were not declared, so R₋ cannot be excluded. Test: compare DAN activity in the consolidation window between the trained group and a stimulus-matched sham-trained group (not against baseline), with a common window statistic frozen in advance and time resolution down to 0–1 h (P19-10, preferred experiment ★4).  
4.6.6  Parallel Opposing Trace-Responses and Same-Synapse Rewriting (Theorem 18-7(5) of \[18\])  
Theorem 19-12 (two bearers of extinction) \[proved; verified Q6n, Q10n\]  
　(1) Parallel opposition: the original Trace-Response d\_N and the opposing Trace-Response d\_P converge on the same read-out, with reading y(n) = 2(d\_N r\_Nⁿ − d\_P r\_Pⁿ); with equal rates and equal depths it is identically 0 (hidden, H2b of \[18\]). The equation y = 0 defines only the zero crossing (in continuous time t₀ = ln(d\_P/d\_N)/(1/τ\_P − 1/τ\_N), with t₀ ≥ 0 when d\_P ≥ d\_N and τ\_P \< τ\_N; in discrete time, the first beat with y(n) \> 0). Experimentally visible spontaneous recovery requires that the original Trace-Response be positive (d\_N \> 0), that an initial cancellation relation apply (y(0) ≤ 0 at the end of extinction, e.g. d\_N = d\_P), that the opposing layer decay faster (r\_P \< r\_N), and that the reading cross the detection threshold τ\_det—taking the max over the sampling beats for a discrete protocol and the sup over the declared read-out window for a continuous protocol (Q10n).  
　(2) Same-synapse rewriting: a signed write directly shallows the original Trace-Response when DAN is below the zero point; thereafter the Trace-Response changes only through a fixed rest law (relaxation ρ ≥ 0, no opposing layer, a rest law whose parameters do not change over time), so it does not rise again after long rest.  
　Proof: (1) Theorem 18-7(5) of \[18\]; the zero crossing is solved from the equality of the two exponentials; visibility follows from the definition. (2) Rest beats have only the relaxation component −ρd ≤ 0. ∎  
　Discriminating power: the absence of recovery is compatible with (2), and also with an opposing branch of equal rate or one that does not cross the detection threshold; the presence or absence of recovery discriminates only between the “fast-decaying opposing branch” and the “frozen same-synapse branch”.  
　Reading: the extinction reported in F10 is of the parallel-opposition type; the extinction effect of F4 falls on the plasticity reading of α3 itself and depends on valence and time point (whether it is borne by the same synapse or by a parallel layer is undetermined). The two mechanisms can coexist in different compartments and under different protocols; the discriminating reading is spontaneous recovery after long rest (P19-13).  
4.6.7  Changes in Connection and Overlap (Theorem 18-9 of \[18\])  
　In the declared models, changes in organizational relations on the neural side extend only to derived incidence (effective overlap is derived from depth, Q3c): when the depression at a shared KC position reaches its limit, that KC no longer passes the drive of the overlapping odor on to the MBON, and the tendency of the overlapping odor changes. The generation and elimination of Structural Positions (formation of new synapses, loss of synapses) has a representational form within the runs of \[18\] (existence variables, Q2i); the *Drosophila* instance has no corresponding material, and this is not declared \[Insufficient\].  
4.7  Step 7  Distilling Dynamics Types and Re-Verifying the Judgment  
4.7.1  Dynamics Types (vocabulary of Section 5 of \[S16\]; the three layers written separately: model item by item / conditional application at the material layer / citation of duties)  
　(Columns: process \| law and type code \| concrete process bearing the historical dynamics \| model layer \| material layer (conditional) \| duty)  
　γ1 write \| third-factor variant of Φ1b (component multiplied by DAN) ⊗g DAN gating (Φ9-type same-beat reinforcement gating) \| coincidence of KC × PPL1-γ1pedc \| 𝒦\_B, 𝒩₃F, Fly \| \[Fact\] synaptic-current layer \| signal source of the training beat.  
　γ1 read-out \| Φ5a threshold read-out (binary) or truncated-linear read-out (graded) \| — \| as above \| \[Fact\] firing layer \| Part.  
　γ1 shallowing \| Φ2 position-wise rate \| direct read of its own position \| as above \| \[Fact\] time course (as reported in F1, F2) \| direct-read-branch witness for HDyn (Q6a).  
　Saturation \| Φ3a clipping \| — \| as above \| \[Fact\] 80%–90% \| —  
　γ1pedc → α3 \| Φ16 cross-subset disinhibition gate \| T₁ → MVP2 drive → PPL1-α3 (U \< 0) → α3 write \| Theorems 19-7, 19-7′, 19-H \| \[Fact\] blockade dependence; \[Mechanism hypothesis\] synaptic layer \| activity-mediated-branch witness for HDyn (Theorem 19-H, Q7a).  
　Same-compartment return \| Φ1b ⊗g(¬g\_M) (inhibitory, continued-writing cell) or ⊗g(g\_M) (excitatory, self-limiting cell) \| T → MBON → DAN → same-compartment write \| Theorems 19-4, 19-5, 19-6 \| γ1pedc: the evoked response of PPL1-γ1pedc to CS+ falls relative to baseline (F3), compatible with the insufficient-disinhibition regime; insufficient disinhibition itself is a model antecedent (P19-8); α1: excitatory (declared reading) \| —  
　Old impedes new \| Φ16 ⊗ overlap \| old Trace-Response on overlapping KCs → MBON → DAN → write of the new odor \| Proposition 19-3 \| old memory impeding the writing of new memory in the α1 compartment, as reported in F6 \| history-condition hyperedge of the organization hypergraph (minimal setting subset).  
　Late weakening \| reverse write at the same synapse (signed) or parallel opposition ∥ \| γ1 already shallow → PPL1-α3 returns to decrease → α3 shallows \| Theorems 19-7 (signed), 19-12 \| \[Fact\] time-point-dependent readings of F4; location undetermined \| —  
　Extinction (parallel-opposition type) \| Φ16 parallel opposition ∥ \| the opposing layer writes a separate opposing Trace-Response \| Theorem 19-12 \| F10 (abstract layer) \| separate declaration χ\_ext.  
　Consolidation \| law-layer write (4.5 of \[18\]: R\_other) \| in-window DAN → consolidation state c → shallowing rate \| FLYC of \[18\], Theorem 19-10 \| \[Fact\] output required 0–1 h after training (behavioral layer); history control or clock control undetermined \| when the law-layer component is a Trace-Response by D18-3, its write is a process acting on T.  
　Common mechanisms (shared with other concretized fields): use-dependent writes, shallowing, saturation, tendency-mediated writes, and organization determining the affected subset (what is shared with the *E. coli*, *Physarum polycephalum*, beach-surface and riverbed instances of 8.1–8.4 of \[17\] and with the *Physarum polycephalum* of 9.3 of \[18\] is the duty structure “existing Trace-Responses take part, through activity, in determining subsequent writes”).  
　Host-specific: the third-factor channel separates transmission activity from the write signal (read-out positions and teaching positions belong to different cell populations); depression-type read-out makes “the deeper, the harder to pass” go together with “the deeper, the more DAN of another compartment is recruited”; one-way cross-compartment gating (the short trace determines the write direction of the long trace, and the long trace does not feed back on the short trace); parallel opposing layers; law-layer consolidation borne by in-window circuit activity (history control or clock control to be determined).  
　All dynamics types are taken from the vocabulary of Section 5 of \[S16\]; the third-factor variant of Φ1b and the coupling notation are registered under the criterion of Section 1 of \[S16\].  
4.7.2  Duty Verdicts at the Model Layer (Column A; Definitions 6′ and 7′ of \[17\])  
　The verdicts for the three Fly variants in 8.6 of \[17\] are used as they stand: Fld is 1 for full, gonly and gfb alike. Members of this paper:  
　　Two-compartment member of 𝒩₃F (under the antecedents of Theorem 19-H): Fld = 1; the activity-mediated-branch witness for HDyn is the α3 write; SED\_W = 1 (Q7a).  
　　Control with the return removed (U₁₂ = 0): Fld = 1, borne only by the direct read of γ1 relaxation; SED\_W absent (Q7a). By Definition 7′ of \[17\], the main verdict does not use SED as a threshold; the presence or absence of SED is a dynamics diagnosis, not a difference in qualification.  
　　The three Consol readings: the history condition of the consolidation write falls on the D branch (permissive type) and on the A branch (return types), respectively (Q6i, Q6j).  
　　Cereb: SED\_W present, SED⁺ absent (Q7c).  
　　Members of 𝒦\_B: the activity-mediated branches of Φ\_sr, Φ\_α^I and Φ\_γα3^0 are given by Theorems 19-5(3), 19-6(6) and 19-7 (Q8); Φ\_γ lies in the insufficient-disinhibition regime and Φ\_α^II has teaching alone sufficient, so they have only the direct-read branch or teaching writes.  
4.7.3  Conditional Argument Covering a Class of Biological Neural Structures  
Theorem 19-H (conditional qualification and historical-dynamics diagnosis on compartmentalized third-factor circuits) \[proved; verified Q7a, Q7a′, Q10a–Q10e, Q11u–Q11w\]  
　Let the declared model of χ be a two-compartment member of 𝒩₃F (short-trace compartment γ, long-trace compartment α) with depression orientation (d = 1 − w/w⁰, MBON read-out falling with depth), and let the signal at re-exposure beat t ∈ W (W the declared evidence window) be unpaired CS+ (a\_i = 1 on K, \|K\| = n\_A). Two arms: actual state σ and control state σ′ (K×{γ} set back to baseline, the rest unchanged); “setting back to baseline” must belong to the declared legitimate joint-setting family. Read-out: m\_γ = R(Σ\_{i∈K}(1 − d\_{γ,i}) − θ\_γ), DAN\_α = Q(β\_α + U·m\_γ), W\_α = η\_α·G(DAN\_α), G(z) = z (unsigned) or z − θ\* (signed), with slope 1 in the affine regime. Antecedents:  
　　N1 (bearing positions): before beat t, every position of K×{γ} has depth d\_γ \> ε, each with a record of formation and retention earlier than t (Definition 6′(B) of \[17\]), and all belong to the same T.  
　　N1′ (the target is a position already left at that beat): the target α position has depth \> ε before beat t and belongs to T. Example X3 (Q10d: α at 1/2 after training, 0 before re-exposure, 3/16 after) does not satisfy N1′, and this theorem issues nothing there (that beat is a new formation). Definition 6′ of \[17\] requires only that the bearing positions be already left before the beat and that the target be x ∈ T; N1′ is an extra restriction imposed by this sufficiency theorem and does not change the upstream definition.  
　　N2 (read-out not on a plateau): the MBON\_γ inputs of both arms lie in the linear regime of R.  
　　N3 (return sign and passage): U \< 0; β\_α + U·m\_γ \> 0 in both arms.  
　　N4 (the control variable does not enter the direct read): the law of W\_α does not directly read K×{γ} (it may read quantities that do not change with the control, such as the α position itself).  
　　N5 (margin): η\_α·\|U\|·Σ\_{i∈K} d\_{γ,i} \> ε; same-beat component \|W\_α(σ)\| \> ε.  
　Conclusions (model mode):  
　(1) Effective difference for HDyn: the α write process has, at beat t, an effective-difference witness in the sense of Definition 6′ of \[17\]—same beat, same process, same target; the control changes only K×{γ}; Δ\_tot = η\_α·U·Σ\_{i∈K} d\_{γ,i}; the route is given by the model structure, and the action is certified by N5; the two branches are A = Δ\_tot, D = 0 (N4). For general monotone R, Q, G (outside the affine regime), only the actual component difference is issued: the effective difference holds when \|W\_α(σ′) − W\_α(σ)\| \> ε, and no slope is given. W\_α may be positive or negative; the negative write of Example X2 (Q10a) (W = −1/16, control −1/8) satisfies (1) equally (Q10b).  
　(2) Fld: further requires the TraceSet of the same T (= K×{γ, α})—an element certificate for every position (Definition 4′ of \[17\]: self-retention lower bound ≥ τ\_W, and K1–K3 relative to the declared probe CS+ and the write condition “paired with reinforcement”), an organization component (Definition 5′), and process carriers within B (Theorem 17-5′(2) of \[17\]). On the organization clause: when T is declared as a complete organization, T must equal exactly one component under the declared probe and joint-setting family; when T is declared as a sub-organization, a separate instance must be set up with fixed outer-position boundaries (the positions of the component outside T frozen at declared values); “T lies within one component” alone does not suffice (Q11w). The three items are supplied separately; having been formed, threshold crossing or a candidate list cannot substitute for them. Under (1) and these three items, Fld(χ) = 1.  
　　Analytic coverage of the member checked (Q10c, Q11u): the member has η\_α = 1/4, U = −1, β\_α = 5/2, θ\_γ = 1/4, ρ\_γ = 1/8, four KCs, A = {0, 1}; γ: η\_γ = 1/2, reinforcement injection 1, β\_γ = 0; α: θ\_α = 1/2, ρ\_α = 0. After one A+ from the zero initial state, the A-position depth of γ is 1/2 and that of α is 3/16; at rest the sequences are (1/2)(7/8)^k for γ and constantly 3/16 for α, so the self-retention (ε = 1/100) is 30 beats for γ (k = 29 is the last beat above threshold and k = 30 the first below; lower bound under Definition 2′ = last above-threshold beat + 1, with the tail given by a closed-form envelope) and infinite for α, both ≥ τ\_W = 6. K2: with the other positions at the ground state, the participation effect of a single-position depth q ∈ \[0, 1\] under probe A equals q (the MBON inputs of both compartments are 2 − q − θ \> 0, in the linear regime), in the same order as depth. K3 (paired with reinforcement): a one-beat write at a γ position is 1/2 ≥ the maximal relaxation ρ\_γ·1 = 1/8, and the write at an α position is ≥ (1/4)(5/2 − 7/4) = 3/16 \> 0 while ρ\_α = 0, so q′ ≥ q for all q ∈ \[0, 1\]. Organization: exact enumeration of joint settings gives the unique component {g1:0, g1:1, a3:0, a3:1} = T (complete organization). Carriers: the formation positions (A×{γ, α}) and the state positions actually read by each process (W\_α reads A×{γ} through MBON\_γ; W\_γ reads only the external reinforcement; relaxation reads its own position) all lie within the declared Structural Positions B.  
　(3) SED\_W: further requires an oriented component W\_α(σ) \> ε (depression deepening) with the A branch effective; when W\_α ≤ ε (negative writes included), SED\_W does not hold through the α write, and (1) and (2) are unaffected (Q10a, Q10b).  
　(4) U = 0 is a separate control domain: the α write is independent of T, and (1) fails for the α write. Fld can then hold through the direct read of γ relaxation, which requires that relaxation exist (ρ\_γ \> 0) and that its component and control difference reach the threshold of Definition 6′ (\|ρ\_γ·d\_γ\| \> ε); when ρ\_γ = 0 and U = 0 there is no certificate on T within the window, which is recorded as ⊥, not 0. Both arms, with and without relaxation, are trained first and keep the actual pre-beat history, and the witness is then restricted to the evidence window after training (Q11v); “α relaxation zero, no other component depending on T” is listed as a condition of that member.  
　Proof: (1) Under N2 and N3, R and Q take the identity branch, and W\_α = η\_α(β\_α + U(Σ\_K(1 − d\_γ) − θ\_γ)) − η\_α θ\*·\[signed\]; the control increases Σ\_K(1 − d\_γ) by Σ d\_γ, so W\_α changes by η\_α·U·Σ d\_γ; N4 makes the direct read of σ paired with y(σ′) already give the entire difference, so D = 0; N1 and N5 give (B), (C) and (D) of Definition 6′. (2) Theorem 17-5′(1)(2) of \[17\]. (3) The definition of SED\_W in Definition 7′ of \[17\]. (4) When U = 0, DAN\_α does not read m\_γ; the control difference of the γ relaxation −ρ\_γ d is ρ\_γ d. ∎  
　Verification: Q7a (60 unsigned members), Q7a′ (20 cases each in which N2 or N5 fails); Q10b (signed branch: 168 positive writes, 25 negative writes; SED\_W if and only if (1) and W \> ε, with 0 violations).  
　Scope of the class (Column B, conditional): 𝒩₃F is meant to cover biological neural structures that satisfy the following three conditions: (A) changes in the efficacy of read-out-position synapses require the coincidence of presynaptic activity and a modulatory (third-factor) signal; (B) the modulatory neurons receive the output of the read-out neurons (directly or through one or two relays), with a definite net sign; (C) the read-out is monotone in efficacy. The insect mushroom body has structural evidence satisfying (A), (B) and (C) (compartmentalized DANs, with MBON→DAN returns visible in the connectome and in functional blockade); vertebrate cerebellar PF→PC LTD structurally satisfies (A), (B) and (C) (the climbing fiber is the teaching signal, and PC ⊣ DCN ⊣ IO is a net excitatory return through two inhibitory stages, 4.7.4); corticostriatal synapses of the basal ganglia have a basis for dopaminergic modulation under (A), but the net sign and pathway of (B) have not been settled, so they are registered only as a candidate (U19-9). The direction law applies only when the actual inputs fall within the declared intervals and the links are effective; the three monotonicities (A)–(C) do not by themselves imply a historical-dynamics certificate, which must be issued separately under all the antecedents of this theorem.  
　For the real χ\_γα3: the theory's claims, bases and tests for each antecedent  
　(Columns: antecedent \| basis (evidence layer) \| prediction of the theory at that point \| test experiment)  
　N1 (γ1) \| F1 voltage clamp; F2 firing time course \| the CS+ depression of γ1pedc under the F2 protocol is of the same type as under the F1 protocol (synaptic layer, odor-specific) \| MVP2 voltage clamp 5 min after training under the F2 protocol.  
　N1 (α) \| F2 MBON-α3 firing depression \> 24 h \| H1: depression of KC→MBON-α3 synaptic efficacy, with absolute drive reduction to the test odor = D·o (P19-6); synaptic-layer write on the re-exposure beat = η\_α\|U\|n\_A(s − s\_m) (P19-1) \| presynaptic KC calcium in the α3 compartment or MBON-α3 voltage clamp; test odors on an overlap gradient (preferred experiment ★5).  
　N2 \| F1: MVP2 still fires after depression \[Fact\]; nonzero firing cannot by itself certify that both arms are in the linear regime, and transfer from the F1 protocol to the F2 protocol needs separate evidence \| H2: truncated-linear read-out, with a straight-line dose–response (P19-1) \| preferred experiment ★1.  
　N3 \| F3: after blockade of MVP2 the training-dependent response of PPL1-α3 vanishes \[Fact\]; connectome MVP2→PPL1-α3 \[Structure\]; negative weight in F11 \[Published model\] \| with other DAN drives controlled and both stages in their working regions, same-trial y\_α3 = β − \|U\|·m\_MVP2 (P19-4) \| dual-color voltage imaging of MVP2 and PPL1-α3.  
　N4 \| F6: optogenetic drive of PPL1-α3 forms long-term memory (behavioral layer) \[Fact\]; F4: the action of extinction on α3 readings depends on valence and time point \[Fact\] \| H3: the write is monotone in PPL1-α3 drive and changes sign at the zero point θ*, with m* = (β\_α − θ\*)/\|U\| (P19-15) \| graded optogenetic drive paired with CS+, with simultaneous measurement of PPL1-α3 voltage.  
　N5 \| — \| the margin η\_α\|U\|Σd\_γ \> ε holds under the F2 protocol: estimated from the slope of P19-1 and the roughly 90% depression of F1 \| slope estimate of preferred experiment ★1.  
　Column B (the theory's claims about the real object): This theory claims (T10) that, with the synapses on the CS+ KC subset in γ1pedc ∪ α3 as T, Fld holds, and HDyn is borne by the cross-compartment activity-mediated write (not by relaxation alone). Its sufficient derivation invokes all the conditions of (1)–(3) of this theorem (retention, element certificates, T equal to one organization component, same-beat actual effective action, route, carriers within B), each holding on the real object; H1–H3 plus a same-beat correspondence cannot sign off these conditions by proxy. The predictions for the individual conditions are the items of the table above together with P19-4, P19-6, P19-10 and P19-15; if they are breached, the claim is withdrawn according to the complete premise set listed in Section 10, and it is not judged 0.  
　Column C: ⊥ (measurement mode; this indicates only that empirical certification has not yet been obtained). Reason codes: ⊥\_Obs (the public records listed in F11—the figure-by-figure Source Data and the Zenodo traces—have not yet been compiled animal by animal under the declared criterion and recomputed; the repository summary table used has only group means and SEM), ⊥\_Chain(C2, C3′), ⊥\_Act (bypass bound of C6 not given). By Theorem 17-10 of \[17\], under the two declared kinds of record the upstream criteria cannot give a verdict directly; the supplementary routes are Theorem 17-8′ and the preferred experiments of Section 10.  
4.7.4  Cross-Host Comparison: The Cerebellum (Structural Comparison) \[verified Q7b, Q7c\]  
　Structural correspondence: read-out-position Trace-Response = LTD at the granule cell (parallel fiber) → Purkinje cell synapse (depression type); read-out = PC firing; modulation = climbing fiber (inferior olive); return = PC ⊣ cerebellar nuclei ⊣ inferior olive. Theorem 19-4 places it in the self-limiting cell.  
　Citation level: Kenyon, Medina and Mauk 1998, per the original abstract: climbing-fiber-dependent granule cell→Purkinje cell plasticity, through LTD and LTP together, self-regulates spontaneous climbing-fiber activity to an equilibrium level, and the synaptic weight vector approaches an equilibrium hyperplane asymptotically. The Cereb of this paper is a reduced member: a single LTD term and a single LTP term give the CF equilibrium η\_p/η and a single fixed point (Q7b), with ledger SED\_W present and SED⁺ absent (Q7c)—these are results for the reduced member, not a formula-by-formula equivalent of the original model, nor a general CF equilibrium value.  
　The blockade effect of P19-16 holds only under a frozen single-feedback bearer; the finite verification covers only the complete return (U19-9).  
　Transfer conditions: the cerebellar CF is an all-or-none complex spike, and the time window of LTD, the conditions for LTP, rebound firing of the DCN and oscillation of the IO are all absent from Cereb; this comparison shows only “the same class, the same theorem, opposite directions”: *Drosophila* γ1pedc→α3 is an inhibitory cross-compartment return (continued-writing cell, SED⁺ holds), while the cerebellum is a net excitatory return (self-limiting cell). No qualification verdict of any kind is issued for the cerebellum.  
5\. Declaration of Candidate Pairs  
5.1  Frozen Working Domain 𝔇₁₉ \[declared\] (the following items are frozen before any reading is taken; they may not be selected backward from the readings to be adjudicated)  
　Host: the N KC→MBON synaptic positions of a single compartment (depth d\_i ∈ \[0, 1\], depression orientation) and the DAN of that compartment. The window signal bg is the odor-free period within the post-training consolidation window, with background activation b\_i ≥ 0 for each KC, the same in both arms.  
　Two arms: a trained arm (depth d^T) and a stimulus-matched sham-trained arm (d^S, the same odor and reinforcer presented unpaired); same strain, same window length, read out in the same time period.  
　Common window statistic: time bins j = 1, …, J within the window and nonnegative weights w\_j (Σ\_j w\_j = 1; for example, an equal-weight mean over time bins) are chosen in advance and shared by both arms. Observable: Δ̄ := Σ\_j w\_j (DAN\_w(d^T(j)) − DAN\_w(d^S(j))), where d(j) is the depth vector at bin j (in-window relaxation can make the depth vary with j).  
　Unit map: sensor reading F = a·DAN + c (a \> 0, c a bias common to both arms), with a and c given by an independent calibration group and their errors counted in r; reading in model units = (F − c)/a. A DAN reading above the detection limit shows only that the signal is visible; it verifies neither sensor linearity nor that the MBON is off its plateau.  
　Error: the model-unit reading error per arm and per bin is ≤ r; the bound on the in-window between-arm difference outside the declared path is ≤ e\_o; the total control error bound is e := 2r + e\_o. e\_o is declared from an item-by-item budget of training differences, state differences and bypass DAN drive differences; the statistical error of routine controls constrains only reading noise and cannot by itself constrain mechanism differences omitted from the budget. When e is a statistical bound, its coverage 1 − α must be declared (for example, a simultaneous confidence bound given by independent replicates).

### 5.2  Candidates and Forward Maps

　P (permissive type, u = 0): DAN\_w = Q(κ).  
　R\_u (return type, u ≠ 0): DAN\_w = Q(κ + u·R(Σ\_i b\_i(1 − d\_i) − θ\_M)); u \< 0 is written R₋ (inhibitory return) and u \> 0 R₊ (excitatory return); R(z) = Q(z) = max(0, z).  
　Guaranteed region: returns with \|u\| ≥ u\_min, where u\_min is the declared minimal detectable return strength (given by independent calibration or by prior design, not chosen from the present readings) and serves as the lower bound of the range of the R candidates. A weak return with 0 \< \|u\| \< u\_min is still a return; it merely lies outside the guaranteed region.  
　The three candidates all close within 𝒩₃F of Definition 19-1 and share the training, background, window and read-out protocols; P and R\_u differ only in whether the in-window DAN reads the existing Trace-Responses through the MBON. A constant return with b = 0 and u ≠ 0 does not read history (Theorem 19-10(1)); it does not satisfy antecedent D1 below and is not in this working domain.

### 5.3  Antecedents (per Time Bin)

　D1 (history reading): position by position d^T(j) ≥ d^S(j), and Δb\_j := Σ\_i b\_i(d^T\_i(j) − d^S\_i(j)) ≥ g \> 0 (training increases depression at the background-activated positions; g is calibrated by an independent depth or MBON reading, not back-inferred from the reading).  
　D2 (only for the R candidates; P does not require a nonzero u): \|u\| ≥ u\_min \> 0.  
　D3 (three working regions, each checked separately): (A) MBON model working region — in every bin the MBON input of both arms satisfies Σ\_i b\_i(1 − d\_i) − θ\_M \> 0; this needs a separate MBON reading or a known threshold calibration, and DAN visibility cannot stand in for its verification; (B) DAN model working region (only for the R candidates) — in every bin the DAN mechanism input of both arms satisfies κ + u·MBON \> 0, proved by an independent check or by an error-bounded lower bound on positive model activity; (C) sensor working region — the unit map is linear over the range of readings used. Sensor linearity does not by itself prove (B).  
　D4 (error): the observed value is Δ̄\_obs = Δ̄ + δ, with \|δ\| ≤ e (at the declared coverage).

6\. Construction of the Distinguishing Observation and the Discrimination Theorem

### 6.1  The Distinguishing Observation

　The distinguishing observation is the common-window statistic Δ̄ of 5.1: the in-window two-arm DAN difference between the trained arm and the stimulus-matched sham-trained arm, summed over time bins with nonnegative weights frozen in advance. Reading envelopes: O\_P = {x : \|x\| ≤ e}, O\_{R₋} = {x : x ≥ m − e}, O\_{R₊} = {x : x ≤ −(m − e)}, with m := u\_min·g; e \< \|x\| \< m − e is the undetermined region.  
Theorem 19-D (Discrimination of DAN Readings within the Consolidation Window) \[proved; verified Q9d, Q10f, Q10g, Q10h, Q11a–Q11e\]  
　(1) Exact values: under D1–D3, P gives Δ̄ = 0; R\_u gives Δ̄ = −u·Σ\_j w\_j Δb\_j, with sign −sgn(u) and \|Δ̄\| ≥ m.  
　(2) Resolution: if m \> 2e, then O\_P, O\_{R₋} and O\_{R₊} are pairwise disjoint; when the error bound holds and the true mechanism belongs to {P} ∪ {R\_u : \|u\| ≥ u\_min}, a reading in O\_P, O\_{R₋} or O\_{R₊} is judged P, R₋ or R₊ respectively, with no misjudgment; when the error bound is a statistical bound, the probability of misjudgment does not exceed α. No verdict is issued in the undetermined region (the reading is recorded as “undetermined” and not merged into any candidate); under the error bound and the antecedents, no candidate in the guaranteed region produces a value in the undetermined region. If m ≤ 2e, this means only that the present envelopes cannot guarantee separation; it does not prove that some frozen member must read the same as P.  
　(3) The antecedents cannot be dropped: failure of D3(A) can produce identical readings — Example X9 (Q11a): θ\_M = 2, b = (1/2, 1/2), d^T = (1/2, 0), d^S = 0, κ = 1, u = −1; the DAN of both arms is 1 (above the detection limit), but the MBON of both arms is on the lower plateau, so Δb = 1/4 while Δ̄ = 0; failure of D3(B) can also produce identical readings — Example X4 (Q10h): κ = 0, u = −1, b = 1/4, and the DAN of both arms is 0; when D1 fails (b = 0), Δ̄ = 0. D2: u = 0 is P; 0 \< \|u\| \< u\_min is still a return, with Δ̄ = −u·Σ w\_j Δb\_j ≠ 0, but it lies outside the guaranteed region, and its reading can fall in O\_P or in the undetermined region (Q11c).  
　Proof: (1) Under P the two arms are identical. Under D3, R\_u takes the identity branch of both R and Q in every bin, and subtracting the two arms gives Δ\_j = −u·Δb\_j; summing with nonnegative weights gives \|Δ̄\| = \|u\|·Σ w\_j Δb\_j ≥ u\_min·g·Σ w\_j = m. (2) Under P, \|Δ̄\_obs\| ≤ e; under R₋, Δ̄ ≥ m, so Δ̄\_obs ≥ m − e; R₊ is symmetric; m − e \> e gives disjointness, and the positive and negative sides keep the two return directions apart; the coverage of a statistical bound directly gives the upper bound on the probability of misjudgment. (3) For the constructions see Q10h, Q11a, Q11c. ∎

### 6.2  Both Sides Nonempty

　The output sets of all three candidates are nonempty, and each is realized by construction: Q10f (1459 cases; under D1–D3, P gives 0 and R\_u gives −u·Δb, 0 mismatches); for the verified member N = 4, b = (1/4, 1/4, 1/4, 1/4), θ\_M = 0, d^S = 0, d^T = (3/4, 3/4, 0, 0), κ = 1, Δb = 3/8, P, R₋ (u = −1) and R₊ (u = 1/2) give 0, 3/8 and −3/16 respectively; Example X10 (Q11b): with b = (1/2, 1/2), θ\_M = 1/4, d^T = (1/2, 0), d^S = 0, κ = 1, g = 1/4, u\_min = 1/2, e = 1/32, the three candidates give 0, 1/4 and −1/8, which fall in the separated envelopes \[−1/32, 1/32\], \[3/32, ∞) and (−∞, −3/32\]. Under the common-window statistic (in-window depth varying with the common relaxation, nonnegative weights), P gives 0 and R\_u gives −u·Σ w\_j Δb\_j with \|Δ̄\| ≥ \|u\|·min\_j Δb\_j (Q11d, 547 cases, 0 mismatches). Readings with error: within the bound, 238 readings with 0 misjudgments (Q10g) and 27 readings with 0 misjudgments (Q11e).

### 6.3  Scope of Commitment

　Theorem 19-D commits only to the three declared candidates, the guaranteed region \|u\| ≥ u\_min and the working domain 𝔇₁₉. The corresponding experiment is preferred experiment ★4 (P19-10); for the real object, e, g, u\_min, a, c and the three working regions are given by independent calibration; these calibrations are antecedents of the experimental reading and have not yet been obtained (U19-11). History control versus clock control, the setting conditions and long-term retention are conditional results (Theorem 19-10) and supplementary protocols (P19-11, P19-12).

7\. Bounds of Misjudgment and Blind Spots  
　(1) Bound of misjudgment: when the error bound holds and the true mechanism belongs to the candidate set, there is no misjudgment under m \> 2e; under a statistical bound the probability of misjudgment is ≤ α. When the error exceeds its bound, misjudgment can occur (Q11e: δ = −7e makes R₋ read as P).  
　(2) Undetermined region: e \< \|Δ̄\_obs\| \< m − e. When it occurs, what is breached is the declared joint “candidate set–antecedents–error bound” (an antecedent may fail, the error bound may fail, or the true mechanism may be a member outside the candidate set, such as a weak return with 0 \< \|u\| \< u\_min); it does not point specifically to the failure of one antecedent.  
　(3) Weak returns outside the guaranteed region: a return with 0 \< \|u\| \< u\_min can fall in O\_P (Q11c); this is a blind spot of this Discrimination Form, not evidence for P.  
　(4) Plateau blind spot: when (A) or (B) of D3 fails, the return type can read the same as the permissive type (Q10h, Q11a); neither DAN visibility nor sensor linearity stands in for verifying that the MBON is off plateau and that the DAN mechanism input is positive.  
　(5) m ≤ 2e: the envelopes intersect and no discrimination is issued; identical readings do not follow (the frozen R₋ member listed in Q11e still has Δ = 1/4, not 0).  
　(6) Constant return: when b = 0 and u ≠ 0, the in-window DAN does not read history (Theorem 19-10(1)), the two-arm difference is 0, and it reads the same as P; it does not satisfy D1 and is excluded by the antecedents, not by the reading.  
　(7) Blockade readings: under the antecedents of Theorem 19-10(5), the impairment of long-term retention by in-window DAN blockade holds under all three readings and does not discriminate the candidates; in-window manipulation of synaptic output (F7) is not a record of an in-window activity difference.

8\. Breach Modes  
　(1) Breach of Theorem 19-D: with every antecedent of 5.1 and 5.3 independently checked and the error bound holding, the reading falls in the undetermined region, or none of the output sets of the three candidates contains the observation — this breaches the declared joint of candidate set, antecedents and error bound.  
　(2) Breach of a conditional theorem: if the conclusion of any conditional theorem (19-2–19-12, 19-H) fails on a declared member on which all of its antecedents hold, that theorem is breached; the appendix programs give only finite verification.  
　(3) Breach of a theoretical claim: each prediction in Section 10 states its breaching observation; a counterexample to a prediction excludes only the complete group of premises (the mapping antecedent MAP, the claims relied on, the read-out assumptions and the difference propagation); locating the failure of one mechanism uniquely requires a separate proof of the remaining premises.  
　(4) “Some effect was not observed” is not rewritten as “there are no historical dynamics”; absence of a difference is a breach only when an absolute lower bound on the effect, given in advance, exceeds the sum of the detection threshold and the control error; a monotone sample that does not cover the target boundary does not refute a mechanism with a jump down at the boundary (P19-12).  
9\. Theoretical Claims and Derivation Chains  
　Each item below is a theoretical claim about the mechanism of the real *Drosophila* mushroom-body circuit (generalized to the class of compartmentalized third-factor circuits). Each is marked “This theory claims” and comes with a derivation chain: starting points (the published facts F1–F11 listed in Section 2.3, at their citation levels; theoretical hypotheses, numbered H) → clauses passed through (\[17\], \[18\] and the theorems of this paper) → results (theorems, predictions). Theoretical derivations are marked \[proved\], in-model verifications \[verified\], and facts reported in the literature \[Fact\]; empirical certification appears only in Column C. Where an upstream antecedent fails, no model verdict is issued, and the claim takes the conditional form “when antecedent X holds, the claim …; the prediction of X itself is ….”  
9.1  Theoretical Hypotheses (Group H; each has breachable consequences, see Section 10)  
　H1  The long-term change in α3 is of the same type as in γ1pedc: third-factor depression of the synaptic efficacy from the CS+-activated KCs onto MBON-α3.  
　H2  The MBON is a truncated-linear read-out of its KC input, and PPL1-α3 is truncated-linear in its MBON-γ1pedc input (graded, not binary).  
　H3  The PPL1-α3 → α3 write law is signed and monotone: when PPL1-α3 activity is above the zero point θ\*, depression deepens; below the zero point, it shallows.  
　H4  The shallowing of the γ1pedc short trace is a first-order (geometric) relaxation with time constant τ\_γ1.  
　H5  Long-term retention is borne by a law-layer component (the consolidation state), whose setting is gated by DAN activity within the post-training consolidation window and requires that the synapse already carry a Trace-Response (a tag).  
　H6  In-window DAN activity is maintained by a drive independent of the Trace-Response (permissive type); competing hypothesis H6′: in-window DAN activity reads the Trace-Response via the MBON return (return type).

### 9.2  Theoretical Claims

Claim T1 (Location and Set of the Trace-Responses)  
　This theory claims: the Trace-Response set of aversive memory in the *Drosophila* mushroom body is the set of depressions of synaptic efficacy from the CS+-activated KC subset onto the MBONs of the compartments; the short trace lies in γ1pedc and the long trace in α3; both are odor-specific synaptic Trace-Responses, neither a change in the intrinsic excitability of the MBON nor a continuation of firing activity.  
　Derivation chain: F1, F2 + H1 → \[17\] Definition 1 (formation positions), 2′ (three-way split; γ1 is a window-length-dependent Trace-Response/residue, α3 is positively determined from a lower bound), 3′ (K1–K3 relative to the probe CS+), 4′ (element certificate), Lemma 17-1′ (firing varies with the Trace-Response but is not a Trace-Response) → Proposition 19-1. \[proved (direct application); verified Q1, Q2a, Q2b\]  
　Resulting prediction: with equal depth at the overlap positions, unit activation of every KC, and the read-outs of both arms in the linear regime, the absolute drop in MBON-α3 drive to a test odor X is Σ\_{i ∈ K(X) ∩ K(CS+)} d\_i = D·o, proportional to the number o of shared KCs (P19-6, \[verified Q9f\]); the proportional form o/\|K(X)\| requires normalized depression or a fixed \|K(X)\|. A uniform-gain change in intrinsic excitability predicts, under matched baseline drive, the same drop (or the same proportion) for every o, and can be distinguished from D·o; a general change in intrinsic excitability can correlate with odor responses, and the overlap curve alone cannot exclude it.  
Claim T2 (Cross-Compartment Gating Law)  
　This theory claims: the post-training write of unpaired CS+ to α3 is an activity-mediated function of the γ1 short trace via MBON-γ1pedc → PPL1-α3 inhibition:  
　　W\_α3 = η\_α·(β\_α − \|U\|·m\_γ1 − θ\*) (within the range where PPL1-α3 is not truncated),  
　where m\_γ1 is the MBON-γ1pedc drive for that CS+. Hence the same-beat write component is linear in m\_γ1 with slope −η\_α\|U\| and zero point m\* = (β\_α − θ*)/\|U\|, and linear in the common γ1 depression depth s with slope η\_α\|U\|n\_A. For m\_γ1 \< m* the write component is positive, at m\_γ1 = m\* it is a zero write, and for m\_γ1 \> m\* it is negative; the actual post-state also passes through clipping: a positive write does not deepen α3 when α3 is already at full depth, and a negative write does not weaken α3 when it is at zero (Theorem 19-7′(3)).  
　Derivation chain: F3, F4, F5, F11 + H1, H2, H3 → Definition 19-1 (𝒩₃F) → Theorem 19-4 (direction law of Theorem 18-5(3) of \[18\]) → Theorem 19-7′(1)(2) \[proved\]; the evidence for the history condition goes through Definition 6′ of \[17\] (activity-mediated branch A = Δ\_tot, D = 0; Theorem 19-H(1)) and Theorem 18-4 and J₁₈ of \[18\] (via the evaluator of \[S2\], HDyn\_F = 1) \[verified Q6c, Q6d, Q6f, Q6g, Q7a\].  
　Resulting predictions: P19-1 (the dose–response line of the same-beat write component; late readings must pass through read-out conversion and difference propagation), P19-2 (the dose–response zero point and the delay scan share the same threshold m*: under discrete sampling the delay scan brackets m* by an interval, \[verified Q9a, Q11f\]), P19-4 (with other DAN drives controlled and both stages in their working regions, the same-trial PPL1-α3 response = β − \|U\|·m\_MVP2).  
　Note on antecedents: if H2 fails and the read-out is nearly binary, the claim reduces to the binary form of Theorem 19-7 (the flip occurs when MVP2 crosses its firing-action threshold), and the dose–response is a step; the prediction of H2 itself is the line of P19-1.  
Claim T3 (Time Law of the Flip)  
　This theory claims (assuming η\_α \> 0, first-order γ1 relaxation s\_k = σ(1 − ρ\_γ)^k, 0 \< ρ\_γ \< 1, and no parameter change): the flip of the action of unpaired CS+ on α3 is determined by the shallowing of the γ1 short trace, not by a clock. Two first beats are distinguished: k₊ is the first beat with no further positive write (W ≤ 0, i.e., m\_γ1 first ≥ m*, i.e., s\_k ≤ s\_m), and k₋ is the first beat with a reverse write (W \< 0, i.e., s\_k \< s\_m), with k₋ ≥ k₊; s\_m = 1 − (m* + θ\_γ)/n\_A is the γ1 depth corresponding to a zero write.  
　(A) Discrete first arrival: for 0 \< s\_m \< σ, k₊(σ) = ⌈ln(σ/s\_m)/ln(1/(1 − ρ\_γ))⌉; for σ ≤ s\_m, k₊ = 0 (the threshold is already crossed at the first beat, which gives only a one-sided bound).  
　(B) Shift law: under the common threshold 0 \< s\_m \< σ, m\* \> 0, 0 \< ρ\_γ \< 1 and no parameter change between the two strengths, σ → c·σ (c \> 1) shifts x := ln(σ/s\_m)/ln(1/(1 − ρ\_γ)) by L := ln c/ln(1/(1 − ρ\_γ)), so k₊(cσ) − k₊(σ) ∈ {⌊L⌋, ⌈L⌉}; when L is less than one beat (or less than the measurement error), equal delays are still consistent with this claim (Example X12: ρ\_γ = 3/4, s\_m = 1/4; σ = 1/2 and σ = 1 both first arrive at beat 1, L = 1/2). The continuous-time form t\_flip = τ\_γ1·ln(σ/s\_m) is issued only under common exponential shallowing, a common τ\_γ1 and a common s\_m.  
　(C) A proof of the tail k₊ = ∞ must list its conditions: σ \> 0, 0 \< ρ\_γ \< 1, s\_m = 0 (m\* is exactly the reachable positive upper bound of the MBON); then s\_k = σ(1 − ρ\_γ)^k \> 0 always holds, and W \> 0 always holds while tending to 0 from the positive side; for σ = 0, k₊ = 0, and for ρ\_γ = 1 the zero write is reached at beat 1, both finite. The other branch, ρ\_γ = 0 (γ1 does not shallow): for σ \> s\_m, W is a constant positive value (k₊ = ∞); for σ = s\_m, W is always 0, “permanently at the zero point” (k₊ = 0, k₋ = ∞), which is listed separately from “tending to zero from the positive side.” If nothing is seen within a finite window, only a lower bound is recorded. Actual deepening and weakening further require that the post-state be changeable (Theorem 19-7′(3)).  
　Derivation chain: T2 + H4 → Theorem 19-7′(2)(3) → Corollary 19-7″ (shift law) \[proved; verified Q9b, Q10k, Q10l, Q11g, Q11h\].  
　Resulting predictions: P19-3 (the shift law, in the rounded form of (B)); the competing clock-control theory predicts that the first-arrival beat does not depend on σ. Conditions for discriminating power: L must exceed the sum of one beat and the measurement error; the binary threshold s₁\* and m\* may coincide or fall in the same sampling interval; only when the two are independently calibrated and their first-arrival intervals separate under sampling does the flip time discriminate the binary reading from the graded reading.  
Claim T4 (Same-Compartment Sign Law and the Regime of Each Compartment)  
　This theory claims: the behavior of each compartment under unreinforced re-exposure is determined by “Trace-Response orientation × net sign of the return from read-out to DAN” (Theorem 19-4): a depression-type Trace-Response continues writing on an inhibitory return and is self-limiting on an excitatory return. Specifically:  
　(A) γ1pedc: MVP2 → MP1 is inhibitory; this theory claims that disinhibition is not sufficient for the PPL1-γ1pedc write to overcome relaxation, so γ1pedc does not continue writing (this is a model antecedent still to be tested: the drop, relative to baseline, in the evoked PPL1-γ1pedc response to CS+ reported in F3 is compatible with it but does not by itself imply it); the criterion for a positive net component is η·DAN(s) \> ρ·s (\[verified Q9h\]), and strict net deepening further requires s \< hi (at full depth s = hi the positive net component is cut off by the upper clip and the post-state remains hi, Q11p); the disinhibition increment DAN(s) − DAN(0) = \|u\|·n\_A·s holds only when the MBON and DAN of both arms are in the linear regime.  
　(B) α3: in F11, MBON-α3 → PPL1-α3 is a positive weight, so by the sign law α3 is self-limiting: with the same γ1 state, other inputs fixed and neither stage on a plateau, the deeper the existing α3 depression, the smaller the same-beat write component on re-exposure, with slope −η\_α·u\_αα·n\_A (\[verified Q9g\]); the slope of late readings also passes through read-out conversion and difference propagation (P19-7).  
　(C) α1: MBON-α1 is glutamatergic and PAM-α1 is NMDA-receptor dependent (F7); if the same-beat return is excitatory, α1 is self-limiting and, within the local mechanism proved in Proposition 19-3 (off plateau, threshold crossing, minimal setting subset), produces an “old impedes new” effect graded with KC overlap.  
　Derivation chain: F3 (the drop, relative to baseline, in the evoked PPL1-γ1pedc response to CS+, Fig. 3e, Extended Data Fig. 5a; this observation does not by itself imply that the write is insufficient to overcome relaxation, which is a model antecedent tested by P19-8), F7, F11 → Theorems 19-4, 19-5, 19-6, Corollary 19-4a, Proposition 19-3 \[proved; verified Q4a, Q4b, Q3d, Q3e\].  
　Resulting predictions: P19-7 (α3 self-limitation slope), P19-8 (γ1pedc does not continue writing; the continued-writing criterion), P19-9 (overlap gradient of the impediment).  
Claim T5 (Reading Is Writing: A Test Is a Write)  
　This theory claims: in the verified declared models, every CS+ test writes to α3 by the law of T2 (the conclusion is limited to the verified models). For the real object this is a conditional inference: if T2 and H3 hold under the protocol of F2–F5, then an early test at delay t changes the subsequent α3 depth by  
　　Δ\_test(t) = η\_α·(β\_α − \|U\|·m\_γ1(t) − θ\*) (when unclipped),  
　which is positive for k \< k₊, zero for k₊ ≤ k \< k₋ (exactly at the zero point) and negative for k ≥ k₋: a test before the flip strengthens long-term memory, and a test after it weakens long-term memory. m\_γ1(t) is the pre-beat reading at that test beat, not an earlier probe reading (an early reading can change, and even reverse sign, through the subsequent probe and rest: in Example X18 the early reading (m, W) = (1/4, 1/8) becomes (13/16, −5/32) after that probe and one rest beat, Q12a). The formula above is a same-beat difference; the difference of delayed readings is in general Σ\_s W\_s·∏\_{j\>s} r\_j (under common linear rest decay r\_j; with no relaxation, no clipping, consistent subsequent reads and writes and linear calibration it reduces to ΣW\_s); if the test changes subsequent writes, consolidation or other read-out components, the actual transfer of the two arms is used (P19-5, P19-14; Example X13: one beat of quiet relaxation turns 1/8 into 1/16, Q11i). Long-term readings that include intermediate tests (such as the 24 h α3 reading of F2) therefore include test writes. A late reading is first written as a depth difference and then converted to observed units by read-out assumption RO1; the behavioral PI, including its sign, must be calibrated independently, and when RO1 is not established no sign is issued. “No difference between the two groups” breaches this claim only when an absolute lower bound on the late-reading effect, given in advance, exceeds the sum of the detection threshold and the control error: being exactly at the zero point (W = 0), dissipation, clipping or cancellation can each give no difference (Q12a, Q12b).  
　Derivation chain: T2, T3 + Theorem 18-3(4) of \[18\] (a probe is a write; non-invasiveness is defined by the actual post-state) \[proved; verified Q5a, Q5b, Q9c: on FLY of \[18\], the difference in final α3 state between the early-test and no-early-test groups is exactly η(−a + b·n\_A·D·r₁^k), which changes sign at k = n\*(D)\].  
　Resulting predictions: P19-5 (magnitude and sign of early-test interference); the competing reading “testing is non-invasive” predicts no difference between the two groups.  
Claim T6 (Consolidation: History-Controlled Law-Layer Write; the In-Window DAN Reading Is Discriminated by Theorem 19-D)  
　This theory claims: the long-term retention of α3 and α1 is borne by a law-layer component (the consolidation state); the consolidation state is set under gating by DAN activity within the post-training consolidation window (history control), and setting requires that the synapse already carry a tag (pre-beat depth \> τ\_tag) and that c\_x still be settable. The in-window DAN reading is discriminated between the permissive type P and the return types R₋, R₊ by the two-arm difference of Theorem 19-D; the preferred claim of this theory is P (\|Δ̄\_obs\| ≤ e, with all antecedents of 5.1 and 5.3 independently checked).  
　Derivation chain: F7, F8 + H5 → D18-11 and Theorem 18-8(6) of \[18\] → Theorem 19-10 (conditional result); in-window reading → Theorem 19-D (load-bearing) \[proved; verified Q6h–Q6m, Q9d, Q10f–Q10j\]. F9 is compatible with P but cannot exclude R₋, and its nonsignificance is not an equivalence test for P: its control is an untrained (vehicle) group rather than stimulus-matched sham training; its data are aggregated by the hour; and it declares neither D1–D4 nor e, m, g. The blockade results of F7 and F8 are compatible with all three readings and support only H5 (in-window DAN output is necessary); F7 is a reference for the early-window dependence of appetitive PAM-α1 and is not transplanted directly as the same window for PPL1-α3.  
　Note on antecedents: if the in-window return is the excitatory return R₊, “deep Trace-Responses are not consolidated” further requires the cross-threshold condition κ + u·b·(N − n\_A) \< θ\_c (Theorem 19-10(4); Example X5: κ = 1, u = 1/2, b = 1/4, θ\_c = 1/2 still consolidates at D = 1); nonmonotonicity of long-term memory in training strength further requires declaring the comparability of training strength → depth and of tag → retention, and setting must first affect at least one relaxation beat (P19-12); history control versus clock control requires freezing the antecedents that setting should occur, that blockade does change setting, and that the read-out is visible (P19-11).  
　Resulting predictions: P19-10 (the two-arm difference of Theorem 19-D, preferred ★4), P19-11 (history control versus clock control), P19-12 (training strength–long-term memory, a conditional prediction).  
Claim T7 (Two Bearings of Extinction Coexist)  
　This theory claims: the weakening of α3 by unpaired CS+ 2 h after training is a signed write at the same synapse (the late branch of T2) and does not recover spontaneously after long rest; extinction under the γ1pedc → γ5 protocol is a parallel opposing Trace-Response, with reading y(t) = 2(d\_N e^{−t/τ\_N} − d\_P e^{−t/τ\_P}), and y crosses the zero point when the opposing layer decays faster; experimentally visible spontaneous recovery further requires crossing the detection threshold τ\_det (written for continuous or discrete protocols), and the time course of recovery is determined by the two time constants and the initial cancellation relation; the same-synapse branch keeps a fixed rest law and does not recover.  
　Derivation chain: F4, F10, F11 (a single weight variable, no parallel opposing layer) + H3 → Theorem 19-12 (Theorem 18-7(5) of \[18\]) \[proved; verified Q6n\].  
　Resulting prediction: P19-13.  
Claim T8 (Overlap Determines Organizational Boundaries and Interference)  
　This theory claims: the organization components of the Trace-Response set are determined by KC overlap and return wiring: the write of an odor that overlaps CS+ reads the old Trace-Response via the return, and when off plateau, crossing threshold and taking the minimal setting subset that makes the difference cross threshold, the two belong to the same organization component; non-overlapping ones are separate, which further requires excluding other return edges and co-participation edges; an organization is signed with the formal elements of Definition 4′ of \[17\]. Within the proved local mechanism, the interference of an old Trace-Response with a new write is monotone in the depth already left at the overlap positions.  
　Derivation chain: F1 (overlap correlation r = 0.90) + Theorem 17-4′ of \[17\], Theorem 18-9(1) of \[18\] → Proposition 19-3 \[proved; verified Q3a–Q3e\]. Resulting predictions: P19-6, P19-9.  
Claim T9 (Class Claim: Compartmentalized Third-Factor Circuits)  
　This theory claims: for every neural structure in which (A) a change in efficacy at read-out-position synapses requires the coincidence of presynaptic activity with a modulatory signal, (B) the modulatory neurons receive the output of the read-out neurons with a definite net sign, and (C) the read-out is monotone in efficacy, the historical dynamics of its Trace-Response set are borne by the activity-mediated branch “read-out → modulation → write,” their direction is determined by Trace-Response orientation × net sign of the return (continued writing or self-limitation), and across compartments the short trace determines the write direction of the long trace. The direction law applies only when the actual inputs fall in the declared intervals and the pathway is effective (no stage on a plateau, gates passable); the three monotonicity conditions (A)–(C) do not by themselves imply the historical-dynamics certificate of Definition 6′ of \[17\], which must be issued separately under all antecedents of Theorem 19-H. Vertebrate cerebellar PF→PC LTD, with its net excitatory return via PC ⊣ DCN ⊣ IO, belongs to the self-limiting cell: in this paper's reduced model Cereb, climbing-fiber activity tends to the equilibrium η\_p/η; this is a consequence of the reduced model, not a general CF equilibrium value.  
　Derivation chain: Definition 19-1, Theorems 19-4, 19-H \[proved; verified Q4a, Q7a, Q7a′, Q7b, Q7c\]. Resulting prediction: P19-16.  
Claim T10 (Duty Layer: Qualification)  
　This theory claims: the real χ\_γα3 is a Sedimentation-Field in the sense of \[S1\] (Fld = 1), and its historical dynamics are borne not only by relaxation but by activity-mediated writes across compartments. Sufficient derivation: invoke all conditions of Theorem 19-H(2) — retention at every position of the same T (Definition 2′), element certificates (Definition 4′), T equal to one component of the declared organization (Definition 5′; a sub-organization requires a separate instance with a fixed outer boundary), effective same-beat actual action (\|W\| \> ε, Definition 6′), and route (activity-mediated branch) and carriers lying in B — each holding on the real object; H1–H3 plus a same-beat correspondence cannot sign for these conditions on their behalf. The testable predictions corresponding to the conditions are P19-6 (elements and overlap), P19-15 (write law), P19-4 (same-beat correspondence) and P19-10 (in-window route).  
　Separate accounts: this is the theory's claim about the real object (Column B); Column A has already given 1 on the declared models; ⊥ in Column C means that empirical certification has not yet been obtained (animal-by-animal raw data are not among the materials relied on, and results of the preferred experiments of Section 10 do not yet exist), not a reservation of the theory.

10\. Breachable Predictions and Preferred Experiments (Column B; none yet tested; not written as confirmed)

### 10.0  Format and General Rules

Each item states: claims relied on \| antecedents \| quantitative form \| experimental protocol (preparation, manipulation, read-out, controls) \| breach (which observation refutes it) \| point of discrimination from competing theories \| in-model values (\[verified\]).  
Competing theories (abbreviated below):  
　C-clk  Clock-controlled consolidation (F11: a time-constant switch 3 h after the last training; consolidation independent of in-window activity).  
　C-fir  Pure MBON firing-layer depression (the long-term change of α3 lies in the intrinsic excitability of the MBON or downstream of it, not in the KC→MBON-α3 synapses).  
　C-nor  No-return model (the α3 write does not read the γ1 Trace-Response; post-training unpaired CS+ has only an action on α3 that is independent of training history).  
　C-bin  Binary gating model (𝒦\_X: read-out and DAN fire by threshold).  
　C-alt  Alternative-route model (the training-dependent part of PPL1-α3 goes through pathways other than MVP2, such as PPL1-α′2α2 or KC adaptation).  
General rules: each prediction takes the mapping antecedent MAP (the real circuit, at the declared temporal resolution, falls in the declared model class) as its first item; a breach excludes the complete group of premises, and locating the failure of one mechanism uniquely requires a separate proof of the remaining premises; “some effect was not observed” is not rewritten as “there are no historical dynamics”; η, β, \|U\|, θ\*, τ in the quantitative formulas are parameters that can be estimated independently in the same preparation, and the strength of the predictions is that the same parameter must take the same value in different experiments (cross-constraint).  
　P19-1 (dose–response line) \| T2 \| H2, H3, PPL1-α3 not truncated; read-out assumption RO1: the map from α3 depth difference to the read-out used (3 h MBON-α3 drive or behavioral PI) is declared (multiplication by n\_A within the MBON linear regime; the quantitative and sign correspondence between behavioral PI and synaptic depth must be calibrated independently, and when it is not established no sign is issued) \| The regressor is the pre-beat m\_γ1 of the 10 min re-exposure beat: the same-beat write component W = η\_α\|U\|·(m\* − m\_γ1) is linear in m\_γ1 with slope −η\_α\|U\|; expressed in γ1 depression depth it is η\_α\|U\|n\_A·(s − s\_m). A late reading is first written as a depth difference — under common linear rest decay r\_j it is W·∏\_{j\>s} r\_j (reducing to W when α has no relaxation, no clipping and consistent subsequent reads and writes, Q10m, Q11i) — and then converted to observed units by RO1 \| Preparation: the voltage-imaging preparation of F2–F5; manipulation: training with 1, 2, 3 or 6 rounds, or graded shocks of 30–90 V; m\_γ1 is obtained in one of two ways, frozen before the experiment: (1) in the treatment arm, read the MVP2 response to that CS+ synchronously during the same 10 min re-exposure, before the write update (measured together with PPL1-α3), adding no such stimulus to the matched arm; (2) if only an early reading at 5–8 min is possible, propagate it to the 10 min beat with a dynamic map calibrated under the common history and budget the propagation error (the early reading and the reading at that beat can differ in sign: Example X18: the early reading (1/4, 1/8) becomes (13/16, −5/32) after that probe and one rest beat, Q12a); the CS+ presentation of the early reading is itself a write event, which is either shared by the two arms and counted in the history or replaced by an independently calibrated group; the MBON-α3 response before re-exposure (the α state) is handled in the same way; MBON-α3 is read at 3 h; controls: at each level, a matched arm without re-exposure (same training, same common probes, same rest, same read-out); a group with MVP2 transmission blocked specifically during re-exposure (the blockade does not by itself imply that the late-reading slope goes to zero, and must be subtracted against the matched arm in the same way) \| Signing condition: the dose–response difference lies above resolution and is read by the exclusion rules for competing explanations (each C below) \| Breach: the depth difference (re-exposure arm − matched arm) increases with that beat's m\_γ1 (wrong sign; requires the sign of RO1 to have been calibrated), or, after conversion through the declared map, it deviates significantly from a line within the declared range (H2 or RO1 is wrong; the two are breached jointly), or the difference in the specific-window blockade group still varies with m\_γ1 (C-alt) \| C-bin predicts a step (a jump when m\_γ1 crosses the threshold); C-nor predicts slope 0 \| \[verified Q6c\] 228 cases in the linear regime, slope always η₂\|U₁₂\|n\_A; \[verified Q10m, Q11i\] difference propagation for the matched arm and for one beat of relaxation; \[verified Q12a\] the early reading and the reading at that beat differ in sign.  
　P19-2 (cross-constraint of a shared threshold; preferred) \| T2, T3 \| as in P19-1, + H4; the sign map from late reading to same-beat component (the sign of RO1) and the difference propagation are established \| The two protocols share the same threshold m*: the zero point m₀ of the P19-1 line estimates m*; the delay scan uses the pre-beat m\_γ1 of the re-exposure beat of each delay group, and only when the antecedents listed above hold does it bracket m\* under discrete sampling by the sign of W back-inferred from the late reading — for k₊ ≥ 1, m\_γ1(k₊ − 1) \< m\* ≤ m\_γ1(k₊); for k₋ ≥ 1, m\_γ1(k₋ − 1) ≤ m\* \< m\_γ1(k₋) (Theorem 19-7′(5)); when the threshold is already crossed at the first beat (k₊ = 0), there is only the one-sided bound m\* ≤ m\_γ1(0); the corresponding reading is issued as the threshold only in continuous time with a transversal crossing \| Two experiments in the same batch: (1) the dose design of P19-1; (2) fixed three-round training, with unpaired CS+ delays of 5, 10, 20, 30, 45, 60, 90 and 120 min and an independent group for each delay; on the re-exposure beat at that delay, read m\_γ1 synchronously as in P19-1 (1) (or propagate as in (2)), and read the direction of α3 plasticity at 3 h (common probes counted in the history) \| The “flip” is reported separately as k₊ (first beat of no further strengthening) and k₋ (first weakening, with every k ≥ k₋ weakening thereafter), distinguishing a first beat exactly at zero, a long-term stay exactly at the zero point, and nothing seen within a finite window \| Breach: with the antecedents listed above established, the confidence interval of m₀ is disjoint from the bracketing interval of the delay scan \| Condition for discrimination: C-bin predicts the flip at the MVP2 firing threshold; the binary threshold and m\* may coincide or fall in the same sampling interval, and only when the two are independently calibrated and the first-arrival intervals of the two candidates separate under sampling does this item bear the discrimination of binary from graded;
